### order-of-simultaneous-abilities
When several players want to resolve abilities at the same time, who goes first?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-19

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-19

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-20

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-17

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-29

theepicwinguy · Community · 2018-01-16disagrees

In the action phase, the active player goes first, then the other players in initiative order counting on from the active player's number and wrapping around. With the active player on 5 and others on 6, 1, 3 and 8, the order is 5, 6, 8, 1, 3. In the strategy and agenda phases, go in order starting with the speaker instead.

### passing-in-a-timing-window
If I pass on playing an action card or ability in a timing window, can I still play one later in that window?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-10

hooliganj · Community · 2018-04-28

germos · Community · 2018-01-15

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-01-26

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-08-26

TerTer · Community · 2021-01-26disagrees

Yes — passing is not final. Players keep taking turns in the window, and if anyone plays something after you passed, you get another chance. The window only closes once every player passes in a row. This applies to riders, Sabotage, combat cards like Skilled Retreat, and any other timed ability.

### resolved-abilities-cannot-be-undone
Once I resolve an ability in a timing window, can I take it back after my opponent responds?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-03-09

timo93 · Community · 2021-03-09

No — anything already resolved stays resolved. Your opponent's response just adds to the window; you can answer it with another ability on your next turn in that window, but you cannot withdraw what you already did.

### two-players-same-after-combat-round-ability
If both players have the same 'after a round of space combat' ability, such as two Exotrireme II dreadnoughts, who resolves first?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

ifaeb · Community · 2025-04-22

hooliganj · Community · 2025-04-22

The active player resolves one first, then the other player, alternating one ability at a time until nobody wants to resolve more. Each use is a separate resolution, so the players take turns rather than one player resolving all of theirs at once.

### ceasefire-after-flank-speed
Can the defender respond to the active player's Flank Speed with a card like Ceasefire?

Best reading — unconfirmed

One experienced player's reading; no official ruling or wider agreement.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-10-09

Yes — both are played after a system is activated, so they share one timing window. The active player gets the first chance to play, then the others in order, and the window stays open until everyone passes in succession.

### simultaneous-game-effects-speaker-decides
How do we order two game effects (not player abilities) that trigger at the same time?

Best reading — unconfirmed

One player relays an emailed FFG ruling; there is no wider confirmation on BGG.

aegiscruiser · Community · 2017-10-12

The speaker decides the order. Such conflicts are meant to be judged case by case, and absent a specific ruling the speaker resolves the timing question.

### then-wording-in-abilities
If an ability says 'then', do I have to resolve the first part before the part after 'then' happens?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-06-13

Somnambulix · Community · 2019-06-17

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-09-08

Clipper · Community · 2019-09-09

Yes — the effect after 'then' only happens if the effect before it was resolved. For example, the Warfare primary gives you no command token back unless you actually removed one from the board. This links effects within a single ability only; a separate paragraph on the same card is its own ability.

### separate-paragraphs-are-separate-abilities
Is each paragraph on a card a separate ability, for example the 'Then return this card' line on a promissory note?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-06-13

Clipper · Community · 2018-04-25

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-10

Yes — each paragraph is its own ability and resolves on its own. So a note's final return line still happens even if an earlier paragraph had no effect, and a paragraph you can resolve still works when a different paragraph can't be done.

### ability-cannot-be-fully-resolved
If an ability tells me to destroy or lose a number of things and I have fewer, do I resolve it partly?

Best reading — unconfirmed

Players disagree; this is the better-supported reading, with no official ruling.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-10

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-10

pkitty · Community · 2017-12-16

GrumblingGamer · Community · 2018-01-10disagrees

Padish · Community · 2018-01-11disagrees

Strictly, no — an ability has to be resolved in its entirety, and only effects joined by 'and' can be resolved separately, so a single effect you cannot fully carry out does nothing. Some players argue for resolving as much as possible. Unstable Planet, for one, was later errata'd to destroy 'up to' 3 infantry.

### living-rules-reference-overrides-old-rulings
If an earlier ruling by the developer contradicts the Living Rules Reference FAQ, which one wins?

Designer ruling

The game's developer answered this directly on BGG in 2018.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-03-07

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-12-17

The Living Rules Reference and its FAQ win. When FFG released it, the developer said anything he had said before that contradicts the FAQ is overruled by it, and the LRR is the definitive current rule set.

“If something I've said before contradicts something in the FAQ, the FAQ is the winner.”

— CreussEmissary, designer

### which-rules-reference-for-base-game
If I only play the base game, which Living Rules Reference should I use?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Ndjcirnc · Community · 2023-02-03

Clipper · Community · 2023-02-03

Asrial · Community · 2023-11-04

Use the current Prophecy of Kings version of the Living Rules Reference and skip the expansion-only parts. It made no major changes to base rules, though rule numbers shifted from v1.3 (59.4 became 68.4, for example). The revised Diplomacy and Construction strategy cards are recommended in base-only games too.

### codex-cards-official-optional
Are the Codex cards official, and do I have to use them?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Surge72 · Community · 2020-11-16

Phoenix1147 · Community · 2020-11-16

hooliganj · Community · 2023-11-10

Clipper · Community · 2022-11-09

Hroost · Community · 2020-06-16

They are official FFG content but optional. Cards marked with the Omega symbol replace the original cards of the same name when you use them; Codex cards without the symbol are additions. The Omega versions are widely recommended, since they were made to fix weak originals.

### showing-hidden-cards
Can I show my action cards or secret objectives to other players?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

dromkaken · Community · 2019-06-28

hooliganj · Community · 2019-06-29

Sande24 · Community · 2022-02-21

AgentDrake · Community · 2022-02-21

hooliganj · Community · 2019-02-23

Yes — you may reveal your own hidden cards to anyone at any time, and they still count as hidden afterwards. The old penalty for revealing a secret objective was removed. This only covers your own hidden components, not hidden information you have merely been allowed to look at.

### sharing-information-from-peeking
After looking at another player's action cards (for example with Mageon Implants), can I tell other players what I saw?

Community consensus

mliszewski · Community · 2019-02-01

Yes — nothing in the rules stops you from sharing or trading that information. Restricting it is a house rule.

### action-card-discard-pile-public
Is the action card discard pile face up, and can players look through it?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2019-02-23

IndyBart · Community · 2019-02-23

Clipper · Community · 2020-02-04

0 hr · Community · 2018-06-06

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-01-16

Clipper · Community · 2019-11-24

Yes to both — discards are face up and public, so anyone may search the pile, for example to count how many Sabotages have been played. Secret objectives have no discard pile; discarded or unchosen ones are shuffled back into their deck.

### destroy-effects-on-own-units
Can I use an effect that destroys units, like Lucky Shot, Unstable Planet or X-89 Bacterial Weapon, on my own units?

No — the Living Rules Reference FAQ rules that abilities that destroy units can only be used against other players' units and planets. Readings that allowed it by the card's wording were overturned by that FAQ entry.

### fighters-are-ships
Are fighters ships?

Per the rulebook — Components and Key Concepts

The rulebook covers this under "Components and Key Concepts".

MollyMae · Community · 2022-01-24

hooliganj · Community · 2019-08-20

NeoTiger · Community · 2019-08-21

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Components and Key Concepts

Yes — the ships are carriers, cruisers, dreadnoughts, destroyers, fighters, war suns and flagships. Fighters count for anything that refers to ships unless it says non-fighter ships. Saar Floating Factories move like ships but are not ships.

### pds-and-space-docks-are-structures
Are PDS and space docks ground forces?

Per the rulebook — Components and Key Concepts

The rulebook covers this under "Components and Key Concepts".

AgentDrake · Community · 2018-12-27

hooliganj · Community · 2018-12-27

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Components and Key Concepts

No — PDS and space docks are structures, a separate unit type. In the base game, infantry are the only ground forces.

### faction-units-count-as-base-type
Do faction units like the Muaat Prototype War Sun count as their base unit type?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2022-08-03

KimAndersen1982 · Community · 2022-08-03

Yes — a faction unit counts as its base unit type for every purpose, so the Prototype War Sun is a war sun. Only the researched upgrade card is a technology: Prototype War Sun II counts as a war sun technology, but the Prototype War Sun I printed on the faction sheet does not.

### faction-sheet-arrows-and-dots
What do the small arrows and the dots by the combat value on a faction sheet mean?

Per the rulebook — Unit Attributes

The rulebook covers this under "Unit Attributes".

TwoAMArts · Community · 2023-05-08

jeffwagner · Community · 2023-05-08

AgentDrake · Community · 2017-11-19

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Unit Attributes

An arrow marks a value that improves when you research that unit's upgrade. Each dot or burst by a combat value is one die the unit rolls, so two dots means two dice.

### fighter-and-infantry-tokens
Are fighter and infantry tokens limited, and what are they for?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Share · Community · 2020-02-21

TerTer · Community · 2020-02-21

Clipper · Community · 2018-11-15

hehaheho · Community · 2021-09-24

McBartsomeville · Community · 2021-09-24

Tokens are unlimited, but each stack of tokens must sit with at least one plastic piece of the same type. So your plastic fighters and infantry limit how many places you can have them, not how many you have. You may swap a plastic piece on the board for a token at any time to free the plastic for use elsewhere.

### infantry-limit-per-planet
Is there a limit to how many infantry can be on one planet?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-08-06

GIIANT · Community · 2020-08-06

No — any number of infantry can be on a planet. You are only limited by your components, and tokens can stand in for extra infantry.

### command-tokens-are-limited
What happens if I would gain a command token but have none left in my reinforcements?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

donmakaron · Community · 2018-07-31

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-07-31

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-08-13

zamoose · Community · 2025-08-13

You don't gain it. Your 16 command tokens are a hard limit. Tokens you spend, such as for a secondary ability, go back to your reinforcements, not to your command sheet.

### must-place-command-token-none-in-reinforcements
What if an effect makes me place a command token from my reinforcements, such as Diplomacy or a retreat, and I have none there?

thomasrobb · Community · 2017-12-25

DauntlessK · Community · 2020-05-02

Marcial · Community · 2020-05-02

You take one of your choice from your command sheet instead. Component actions are different: one that cannot be fully resolved cannot be played at all, so it can't target you if it needs a token you don't have.

### taking-units-from-board-when-out
If my reinforcements are empty, must I take units from the board to place a new one?

Kinan_rod · Community · 2019-06-17

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-17

No — the rules let you remove a unit from the board to place it elsewhere, but you are not required to. This matters for effects such as L1Z1X Assimilate or Swords to Plowshares.

### choosing-factions-colors-seats

However your group likes — the rules deliberately leave the method open. Free choice, random draw, or dealing each player a few factions to pick from are all fine. If you pick in turn, go clockwise from the speaker, as with other unspecified orders.

### look-at-dealt-system-tiles
When building the galaxy, can I look at my dealt system tiles and choose which one to place?

Yes — the dealt tiles are effectively a hand. You see them all and pick which tile to place and where on each turn, within the placement restrictions, which lets you put poor systems near your opponents.

### anomaly-and-wormhole-placement-restriction

Anomalies may only go next to another anomaly when you have no other legal option, and holding any non-anomaly tile counts as an option. Matching wormholes follow the same rule. Nothing stops an anomaly from going next to Mecatol Rex.

No — only tiles with a red border on the front are anomalies. The red back only matters for dealing, so empty systems may go next to anomalies.

### five-player-starting-trade-goods
In a five-player game, which players get starting trade goods?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

MonkeyKnifeFight · Community · 2017-08-29

Fadeout4 · Community · 2017-08-29

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-26

MollyMae · Community · 2019-10-28

Avemo3 · Community · 2018-02-10

csouth154 · Community · 2019-10-26disagrees

In the standard five-player layout, the players whose home systems sit closer to their neighbors receive trade goods as compensation; the others get none. Since the galaxy can't be built until home positions are known, decide who sits where (and so who gets them) first. The Mentak can't pillage these, since nobody is a neighbor during setup.

### faction-technologies-at-start
Do factions start with their faction technologies, and does adding them remove any generic technologies?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2025-08-14

Dulkal · Community · 2019-12-30

Volverbot · Community · 2019-12-30

No to both. Faction technologies must be researched or otherwise gained; you only own the starting technologies listed on your faction sheet. The Nekro Virus is the exception, since its two Valefar Assimilator technologies are listed as starting technologies. Non-unit faction technologies, such as the Xxcha's, are simply added to your deck without removing anything.

### creuss-home-system-setup

Place the Creuss Gate (tile 17) where a home system would normally go and keep the Creuss home system tile in your play area, off the map but visible. Your starting units go in the home system tile. The Creuss Gate has no planet and so no planet card; the card is for the planet Creuss.

Only your lower-numbered card sets your initiative. You still take one turn at a time like everyone else, never two in a row, and you may use either card first.

### strategy-primary-costs-no-token
Does a strategy card's primary ability cost a command token, and does the Leadership secondary?

Per the rulebook — Strategic Action

The rulebook covers this under "Strategic Action".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Strategic Action

Truji · Community · 2025-03-14

Cacotopos · Community · 2025-03-15

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-02-04

jeffwagner · Community · 2021-02-04

No to both. A primary ability never costs a token. Secondaries cost a strategy pool token, except Leadership's, where you just spend influence: 1 command token per 3 influence.

### strategy-card-abilities-mandatory-parts
Which parts of a strategy card's primary ability are mandatory?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

hooliganj · Community · 2025-10-12

Falkman · Community · 2025-10-13

selwyth · Community · 2020-03-24

McBartsomeville · Community · 2023-01-26

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-06-06

Resolve the card top to bottom and do everything it says unless it uses 'may' or 'or'. Leadership's 3 tokens, choosing a Diplomacy system, Trade's replenish (for you and the players you pick), removing a token with Warfare if you can, the first Technology research and naming a new speaker with Politics are all required. Extras that cost you something, such as more Leadership tokens or a second technology for 6 resources, are optional, and Imperial never forces you to pay to score.

### leadership-no-redistribution
Does Leadership let me redistribute the command tokens already on my sheet?

Best reading — unconfirmed

One experienced player's reading; no official ruling or wider agreement.

hooliganj · Community · 2019-01-26

No — you only place the newly gained tokens in any pools. Redistributing existing tokens happens in the status phase and with the Warfare primary.

### diplomacy-revised-card
How does the revised Diplomacy strategy card work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

pkitty · Community · 2020-05-18

andrewvcook · Community · 2020-05-12

Clipper · Community · 2020-05-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-05-12

zamoose · Community · 2018-09-18

manatee_x · Community · 2022-07-15

The revised card (in the Living Rules Reference errata, later printings and Prophecy of Kings) lets the primary player also ready up to 2 exhausted planets they control, giving them the same refresh as the secondary. The secondary readies up to 2 exhausted planets you control for a strategy token. The older section text in the LRR wasn't updated, but the errata governs. The revised card is recommended for base-only games too.

### diplomacy-effect-on-other-players
What do the Diplomacy tokens do to other players?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

donmakaron · Community · 2019-03-08

rosie_187 · Community · 2019-03-08

McBartsomeville · Community · 2021-08-10

Dulkal · Community · 2018-01-04

Jagokok · Community · 2021-06-20

Each other player places a command token from reinforcements in the chosen system, so they can't activate it for the rest of the round. Nothing moves, and the system is not activated. A player who already has a token there doesn't add another. A player with none in reinforcements takes one from their command sheet.

### removing-diplomacy-tokens

Sande24 · Community · 2018-01-04

Yes — once placed it is an ordinary command token, and the game doesn't track how it got there. Warfare, Counterstroke, Unexpected Action and similar effects can remove it.

### politics-choosing-speaker
Who can I make speaker with Politics, and is it mandatory?

Per the rulebook — 1. Strategy Phase

The rulebook covers this under "1. Strategy Phase".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 1. Strategy Phase

DrewDecimal · Community · 2019-01-02

csouth154 · Community · 2019-01-20

AgentDrake · Community · 2019-01-21

Jimb0v · Community · 2017-10-12

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-09-01

You must choose a new speaker, and it must be someone other than the current speaker; that can be you if you aren't already the speaker. The speaker token doesn't rotate on its own and only changes hands this way, so if nobody takes Politics the speaker stays the same.

### selling-the-speaker-token
Can the speaker token be traded or sold?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-08-05

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-08-05

hooliganj · Community · 2024-04-19

Marcial · Community · 2024-04-19

Marcial · Community · 2020-01-26

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-16

Not in a transaction — it isn't a tradeable component. But the Politics player can take payment, or make deals, in exchange for naming someone speaker. Only trade goods, commodities and promissory notes can change hands, and a payment is a transaction, so it must be with a neighbor at that moment.

### warfare-secondary-home-system

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Spend a strategy token to use the Production of one space dock in your home system. It doesn't activate the system and works even if your home system already has your token; if it doesn't, the new ships can still move out later that round. Only one dock can be used, so a Saar player whose docks have left home can't use it.

### one-action-per-turn
How many actions do I take per turn, and in what order must I use action types?

Per the rulebook — 2. Action Phase

The rulebook covers this under "2. Action Phase".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 2. Action Phase

Dulkal · Community · 2020-06-07

Clipper · Community · 2020-06-07

Dulkal · Community · 2017-12-13

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-21

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-08-08

Exactly one action per turn — strategic, tactical or component — or you pass. You may use them in any order over the round and repeat types on later turns; the only restriction is that you can't pass until you have used your strategy card (both cards in three- and four-player games).

### tactical-action-steps-optional
Do I have to move ships or do anything after activating a system?

Per the rulebook — Tactical Action

The rulebook covers this under "Tactical Action".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Tactical Action

Dulkal · Community · 2020-06-07

ChimBlade · Community · 2022-05-23

IncrediSteve · Community · 2024-01-08

selwyth · Community · 2023-06-04

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-09-24

No — only placing the command token is required. Every later step is optional (combat aside, which is mandatory if both sides have ships), so you may activate a system just to fire Space Cannon, to stall, or even one nobody can move into. All the steps happen on that one turn, in order, before play passes on.

### cannot-activate-system-with-own-token
Can I activate a system that already contains my command token?

Per the rulebook — Tactical Action

The rulebook covers this under "Tactical Action".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Tactical Action

pkitty · Community · 2020-08-09

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-19

No — however the token got there (your earlier activation, Diplomacy, Construction's secondary), you can't activate that system again until the token is removed, for example by the status phase, the Warfare primary or Unexpected Action. Other players' activations of nearby systems can still trigger your Space Cannon.

### one-system-per-tactical-action
How many systems can I activate in one tactical action, and why do I end up with tokens in many systems?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

christhomas226 · Community · 2021-03-25

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-03-15

zamoose · Community · 2018-10-26

One. Each tactical action spends one tactic token to activate one system. Your tokens accumulate on the board because the action phase goes around the table many times, and they stay until the status phase. Ships in, or produced in, a system holding your token can't move out for the rest of the round.

### system-active-only-during-action

No — it is the active system only for that tactical action. Your command token stays there until the status phase (or until an effect removes it), which is what stops you activating it again.

### never-two-own-tokens-in-system
Can a system ever hold two of my command tokens?

No — if an effect would place your token in a system that already has one, you don't place a second. Construction says the spent token goes to your reinforcements instead, and other effects work the same way.

### what-counts-as-activating
Do Diplomacy tokens, retreats or action cards like Ghost Ship or Plague count as activating a system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Cacotopos · Community · 2021-01-25

zamoose · Community · 2019-09-19

AgentDrake · Community · 2023-01-04

pkitty · Community · 2022-09-05

No — a system is activated only by placing a command token as the first step of a tactical action. Tokens placed by Diplomacy or a retreat and cards aimed at a system don't activate it, so they don't trigger Space Cannon Offense, Support for the Throne returns or other 'activate' effects.

### move-fight-invade-then-produce
Can I move into a system, fight, invade and then produce there in the same tactical action?

Per the rulebook — Tactical Action

The rulebook covers this under "Tactical Action".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Tactical Action

christhomas226 · Community · 2020-07-06

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-08

GrumblingGamer · Community · 2018-12-30

Yes — the steps run activation, movement, space combat, invasion, then production. Because production comes last, units produced in that action can't join its combat or land on planets that turn. Ships you moved in count toward your fleet pool when you produce.

### only-active-player-produces

No — only the active player resolves the steps of a tactical action. Other players join in only through things like Space Cannon and combat. To build there, activate it on your own turn.

Not with a tactical action, since you can't activate it. Only effects that don't need activation, such as the Warfare secondary in your home system, let you produce there.

### when-to-take-strategic-action

No to both. You choose which turn to take your strategic action, as long as it is before you pass. Strategic and component actions need no command tokens, so a player out of tokens can still play their remaining strategy card and then pass.

### secondaries-resolve-immediately

Right away, clockwise from the active player, as part of the active player's strategic action — it doesn't use their turns. Afterwards play passes to the next player in initiative order. End-of-turn effects can't be slipped in between the primary and the secondaries.

### start-of-turn-abilities
When do 'at the start of your turn' abilities happen, and do they work in other phases?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-06-14

Clipper · Community · 2021-06-14

Clipper · Community · 2020-01-08

Before you choose your action or pass, so they come before activating a system and before anything activation triggers, like Ceasefire. They only exist in the action phase: you don't have turns in the strategy, status or agenda phases.

### component-actions-no-round-limit
Are component actions limited to once per round?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

No — each one simply uses your action for the turn. The only limits are those printed on the component itself.

### component-action-must-fully-resolve
Can I play a component action that I can't fully resolve?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-13

zamoose · Community · 2025-11-02

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-08

Padish · Community · 2018-01-11

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-26

No — a component action can only be performed if you can carry out the whole ability. You can't play Signal Jamming on a player with no tokens in reinforcements, use Sling Relay with nowhere to produce, use Uprising on an exhausted planet, or stall with Yssaril's ability without a card to discard. Unstable Planet is different, because its effects are joined by 'and'.

### action-card-with-zero-effect

AgentDrake · Community · 2019-09-16

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-09-18

Yes — doing the card's text with a result of zero still counts as resolving it, and 'up to' includes zero. Players relaying an official reply confirmed Plague for zero. You must still meet any targeting requirement and do every part that is possible.

### removing-own-units

No — units leave the board only through the game (destroyed, captured and so on). The one exception is taking a unit off the board when you need to place one and none are in reinforcements.

### command-tokens-in-status-phase

No — tokens on the board go back to your reinforcements, and you then gain just 2 tokens to place on your command sheet. Spent tokens are not refunded, so to grow your supply you need Leadership or similar effects. Newly gained tokens may go in any pool.

### agenda-phase-timing-and-readying

Santiago · Community · 2017-12-04

Johnnymaxx · Community · 2017-11-28

Once the custodians token is off Mecatol Rex, the agenda phase follows the status phase every round. Planets ready in the status phase, so they can vote, but not between the two agendas: influence you spend on the first vote is gone for the second. Planets ready again at the end of the agenda phase for the next action phase.

### how-voting-works
How does voting work, and does it go around the table more than once?

Per the rulebook — 4. Agenda Phase

The rulebook covers this under "4. Agenda Phase".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 4. Agenda Phase

MollyMae · Community · 2019-02-06

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-02-06

HP12C_guy · Community · 2024-04-14

hooliganj · Community · 2024-04-14

CBpegasus · Community · 2017-09-13

Each player votes once per agenda, openly, starting to the speaker's left and ending with the speaker. On your turn you name an outcome and exhaust planets for that many votes, or abstain. Votes are committed as they are declared.

### vote-with-part-of-planet-influence
Can I spend only part of a planet's influence when voting?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

MollyMae · Community · 2019-10-28

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-10-28

No — exhausting a planet casts all of its influence as votes. You can only fine-tune your total by choosing which planets to exhaust.

### trade-goods-cannot-vote
Can trade goods be spent to cast votes?

Per the rulebook — 4. Agenda Phase

The rulebook covers this under "4. Agenda Phase".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 4. Agenda Phase

Kamakaze · Community · 2017-08-16

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-08-16

No — trade goods count as influence for everything except voting. You can still pay them to other players to sway their votes.

### abstaining-and-zero-votes
Can I abstain, and does casting zero votes let me use Distinguished Councilor or Bribery?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

StrangeBeacon · Community · 2026-02-16

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-06-22

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-02

ksfga · Community · 2018-03-02

Clipper · Community · 2021-06-12

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-17disagrees

You can always abstain, whether or not you have votes available. Casting zero votes is the same as abstaining, so cards that add to votes you cast, like Distinguished Councilor, need at least one real vote first; a single 1-influence planet is enough.

### ties-and-everyone-abstaining
What happens if the vote ties or everyone abstains?

Per the rulebook — 4. Agenda Phase

The rulebook covers this under "4. Agenda Phase".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 4. Agenda Phase

jeffwagner · Community · 2018-01-05

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-05

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-05

Old_Man_gi · Community · 2022-01-18

denmla · Community · 2018-01-06disagrees

The speaker picks which tied outcome resolves; at 0 to 0 that means any eligible outcome. The agenda is never discarded for lack of votes. The speaker's choice is not a vote, so it doesn't count as voting for that outcome, but riders that predicted it still pay out.

### speaker-who-cannot-vote-breaks-ties
Can a speaker who can't vote, such as the Nekro Virus or a speaker who played a rider, still break ties?

hooliganj · Community · 2018-12-16

Yes — breaking a tie isn't voting, so the speaker always does it.

### agenda-outcome-applies-to-whom

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

zamoose · Community · 2019-01-05

Everyone, unless the card itself limits the effect to players who voted a certain way, as Mutiny does. Abstaining doesn't exempt you.

Clipper · Community · 2020-01-12

Marcial · Community · 2020-01-12

Ender02 · Community · 2023-02-08

ajmarshall · Community · 2023-02-08

If a law's For (or elect) outcome wins, the card stays in play as a lasting effect, even if part of it is one-time. A law's Against outcome, and either outcome of a directive, resolves once and the card is discarded.

### elect-planet-agendas
How do 'Elect Planet' agendas work, and can an uncontrolled planet be chosen?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-05-02

IndyBart · Community · 2020-03-09disagrees

There's no nomination step: on your vote you back an eligible planet someone already named, name a new one, or abstain. Only planets some player controls are eligible, and only those that already have the required trait, since electing never changes a planet's trait.

### when-riders-are-played

Riders go after the agenda is revealed and before the first vote is cast. Talking and negotiating don't close that window, and the speaker can't rush to the vote to shut riders out: everyone gets their chance to respond.

### when-effects-before-after-effects
Do 'when an agenda is revealed' effects like Veto and Quash come before Riders?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-04-27

hooliganj · Community · 2018-04-28

maverikki · Community · 2017-12-21

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-21

pkitty · Community · 2018-03-08

Clipper · Community · 2019-09-05

Yes — 'when' effects (Quash, Veto, Political Favor, Political Secret) resolve first, then 'after' effects (Riders, Assassinate Representative, Galactic Threat), then voting. Veto and Political Favor were errata'd to 'when' timing to match Quash, so they cancel an agenda before anyone has played riders on it.

### rider-playing-order
In what order do players play riders and other agenda-phase cards?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-13

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-14

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-10-21

Clipper · Community · 2018-06-06

MollyMae · Community · 2019-05-19

Ragnarwarbar · Community · 2019-10-15disagrees

Starting with the speaker and going clockwise, each player plays one card or passes, and it keeps going around until everyone passes in a row. Passing doesn't lock you out: if someone plays a card afterwards, you can still play one.

### riders-are-public
Do I have to reveal my Rider and its prediction when I play it?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Yes — action cards are read aloud and played openly, so the whole card, reward included, and your prediction are public. The prediction is made when you play the card, before voting, never after the result is known.

### multiple-riders-one-agenda

### bribery-rules
Who can play Bribery, and when?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2019-09-24

Clipper · Community · 2019-09-25

Bautazar · Community · 2018-03-06

DrewDecimal · Community · 2020-06-05

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-15

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-16

Only a player who actually voted. Per its errata, Bribery is played after the speaker votes and each trade good spent adds a vote to the outcome you voted for. A player who played a rider can't vote and so can't use it, nor can a speaker who abstained, but others who voted still can. If an Argent speaker votes first with Zeal, Bribery comes right after that vote.

Both only work on 'Elect Player' agendas, and only the player originally elected can play them. They act before the outcome resolves, so riders and agenda-phase secrets like Drive the Debate use the final elected player.

### political-secret-what-it-blocks

For that agenda, they can't vote, play action cards, or use optional abilities, those with 'may' or another choice. Mandatory and passive abilities still apply, such as Saar's Scavenge, Nekro's Propagation or Hacan's Arbiters, and laws they own still work.

### political-secret-vs-quash

No — discarding the agenda counts as resolving it, so Political Secret ends and is wasted. The replacement is handled from scratch: it can be Quashed in turn, and players can make fresh transactions for it. Since they share timing, Xxcha may also Quash before anyone plays Political Secret on them.

### exhausting-planets-in-agenda-phase

Yes — nothing limits planets to voting then. You can spend them on things like Inheritance Systems or research from a Technology Rider, but those planets then can't vote. Exhausted cards other than planets don't ready at the end of the agenda phase.

### law-victory-points-kept-when-discarded
If Shard of the Throne, The Crown of Emphidia or Holy Planet of Ixth is discarded or re-elected, does the previous owner lose its victory point?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

THLY · Community · 2019-10-02

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-02

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-08

Ressless · Community · 2024-05-02

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-20

Santiago · Community · 2019-10-02disagrees

No — a point from these laws is lost only through the condition printed on the card. If the law is discarded some other way, such as Repeal Law, or re-elected via Miscount Disclosed, the old owner keeps the point; a newly elected owner gains one, and the same player re-elected scores again.

### crown-of-emphidia-home-system-only
Do I lose The Crown of Emphidia if I lose a planet I control in another player's home system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

TomBoombs · Community · 2021-08-31

No — only planets in your own home system count for it. Likewise, A New Constitution only exhausts the planets each player controls in their own home system.

### ties-for-most-or-fewest-points
If players are tied for the most or fewest points when Seed of an Empire resolves, who scores?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Weltenreiter · Community · 2018-06-25

aegiscruiser · Community · 2018-06-25

Clipper · Community · 2018-06-25

ajmarshall · Community · 2021-09-03

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-03

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

Every tied player gains the point. Effects aimed at the player with the most or fewest points hit everyone tied; the speaker doesn't break this kind of tie. Trading Support for the Throne after the votes can change who qualifies before it resolves. Under Colonial Redistribution, by contrast, the planet's controller chooses one player among those tied for fewest points.

### miscount-disclosed-law-stays-in-effect
When Miscount Disclosed forces a revote on a law, does that law stay in effect during the revote?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

TomBoombs · Community · 2021-08-20

kaiteinomen · Community · 2019-08-06

Clipper · Community · 2019-08-06

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-20

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-14

MercifulBiscuit · Community · 2019-01-23disagrees

Yes — the law remains in play until the revote resolves, so Representative Government, for example, still limits everyone to 1 vote. The old outcome is replaced only when the new one resolves, so a new Demilitarized Zone planet replaces the old one rather than adding a second.

### miscount-disclosed-procedure
How does the revote from Miscount Disclosed work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-05-10

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-10

TomBoombs · Community · 2019-01-23

Marcial · Community · 2021-09-02

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-02

First vote on Miscount Disclosed, with riders predicting only which law is chosen. Then vote on that law as if it had just been revealed, so Veto, Quash and a fresh round of riders can be played on it. Planets are not readied in between, and the revote doesn't replace the phase's second agenda, so a phase can have three votes.

### classified-document-leaks
How does a secret objective made public by Classified Document Leaks work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-11-09

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-11-09

Xelvonar · Community · 2017-12-27

Bizud · Community · 2017-12-28

hooliganj · Community · 2019-01-24

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

While leaked it is a public objective and no longer counts toward its owner's secret objectives, so they may hold another secret. Nobody can score it twice, its original owner included. If the law is later discarded, the card returns to its owner as a secret, and anyone who scored it keeps the point.

### enforced-travel-ban
What does Enforced Travel Ban do, and does it stop Space Cannon or the Creuss?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

pkitty · Community · 2017-12-16

dheck · Community · 2019-04-08

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

alercah · Community · 2020-09-27

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-01-02

For: alpha and beta wormholes have no effect on movement, but the systems stay adjacent for everything else, so Space Cannon still fires through them, as the errata confirms. It also cancels the adjacency from Lost Star Chart for movement. The Creuss ignore it, since no game effect can prevent Quantum Entanglement. Against: every PDS in or next to a system with any wormhole, gamma and delta included, is destroyed.

### ixthian-artifact-adjacency
Which units does The Ixthian Artifact destroy?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-18

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-13

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

Milty · Community · 2019-09-12

zamoose · Community · 2021-08-12

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-12

All units in the Mecatol Rex system, ships included, and 3 units of each player in every adjacent system (all of them if a player has fewer). Adjacency is the normal kind for everyone: a matching wormhole on Mecatol counts, but Creuss's Quantum Entanglement doesn't extend it. With Wormhole Reconstruction in play, alpha and beta systems both count.

### arms-reduction-scope
Does Arms Reduction touch ships other than dreadnoughts and cruisers, and what about capacity?

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

No — For only cuts dreadnoughts beyond 2 and cruisers beyond 4. Afterwards check capacity; fighters and ground forces that no longer fit are removed, which is not the same as being destroyed.

### fleet-regulations-excess

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-05-01

Excess fleet tokens return to reinforcements; you can't move them to other pools. Any ships over the new limit must be removed immediately. Abilities that raise fleet limits, like Letnev's, still add on top.

### anti-intellectual-revolution

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

eurofabio · Community · 2018-01-15

McBartsomeville · Community · 2022-01-28

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-01-29

For: after you research a technology you must destroy one of your non-fighter ships; fighters don't count. It is an after-research effect, not a cost, so a player with no such ships can still research. The Nekro are unaffected, since Propagation gains tokens instead of researching. Against applies only at the start of the very next strategy phase, then the card is discarded.

### committee-formation-timing

Just before voting, after the 'when' and 'after' windows, so riders are already down. The agenda it settles still counts as one of the phase's two. Riders that predicted the chosen player pay out, so playing a rider and then electing that player works. The Nekro can hold and use it despite not voting, and Political Secret doesn't stop it.

### crown-of-thalnos-rerolls

Only combat rolls, not Anti-Fighter Barrage, Bombardment (L1Z1X Harrow included) or Sardakk's Courageous to the End. You may reroll units that look doomed, because hits aren't assigned until rolling ends; any unit whose reroll fails to hit is destroyed, even if its other dice hit.

For Wormhole Research, ships in any wormhole system, gamma and delta included, qualify, but each player researches at most one technology; only ships in alpha or beta systems are then destroyed. Wormhole Reconstruction's Against outcome hits every wormhole system, delta included, which a player confirmed with the developer as intended.

### minister-cards-details
How do Minister of Commerce, Industry, Peace and War interact with other effects?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

Marcial · Community · 2021-08-14

Minister of Commerce still pays 1 trade good per neighbor when your commodities were already full, since you still replenished. Minister of Industry lets you produce in that system right after placing the dock. Minister of Peace's owner needn't have units in the activated system. Minister of War isn't an action itself, so with Fleet Logistics you can take three actions in a turn.

No — if neither player has ground forces left, nobody won the combat, so the Shard stays put. It also never moves through combats that don't happen, such as landing unopposed or bombarding the last defenders.

### research-team-agendas

You exhaust the Research Team card, not the planet, to skip a prerequisite, so the planet can still be spent. They don't give the planet a technology specialty, so they don't count for objectives about specialty planets.

### agenda-phase-secret-scoring-order
In what order are agenda-phase secret objectives scored?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-12-19

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-19

In speaker order, clockwise; it only matters if several players would win from the points, a tie that also goes clockwise from the speaker.

### prophecy-of-ixth-discard
When must The Prophecy of Ixth be discarded?

Best reading — unconfirmed

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-08

Only when you use a unit's Production and don't produce 2 fighters with it. Skipping production, or placing units by other effects like Integrated Economy, keeps the card.

### home-system-needed-for-public-objectives
Can I score objectives if another player controls a planet in my home system?

Per the rulebook — Objectives and Victory Points

The rulebook covers this under "Objectives and Victory Points".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Objectives and Victory Points

Not public objectives — you must control every planet in your home system to score one, and the Imperial card doesn't override that. Everything else still works: secret objectives, Imperial's Mecatol Rex point, agendas, relics, Support for the Throne and the custodians token. Enemy ships blockading your home system don't stop you; only losing a planet there does.

### spend-objectives-pay-when-scoring
For objectives that require spending resources, influence, tokens or trade goods, does earlier spending count?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

zamoose · Community · 2020-07-02

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-09-28

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-07-25

Clipper · Community · 2019-01-05

No — you pay the full amount in one go when you score the objective, as its cost; nothing spent earlier in the round counts. Trade goods can stand in for resources or influence there, though a 'spend trade goods' objective needs actual trade goods. If you score one through Imperial, you pay right then in the action phase.

### must-meet-objective-when-scoring
Can I mark an objective as soon as I meet it and score it later, or keep a point if I stop qualifying?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

isterbat · Community · 2025-07-17

Marcial · Community · 2019-10-02

RedEyedGhost · Community · 2019-10-02

You must qualify at the moment you score. Status-phase objectives check the condition then, so a blockade you lifted earlier doesn't count for Cut Supply Lines. Once scored, points are kept even if you stop qualifying; only effects that say so, like Support for the Throne returning, take points away.

### public-objectives-stay-in-play

Yes — they stay for the rest of the game unless an effect removes them. Any number of players can score each, but each player scores a given objective only once.

### action-phase-secret-objectives
How and when are action-phase secret objectives scored?

Per the rulebook — Objectives and Victory Points

The rulebook covers this under "Objectives and Victory Points".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Objectives and Victory Points

Cacotopos · Community · 2021-02-28

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-02

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-09

MollyMae · Community · 2019-06-26

Secret objectives marked Action Phase are scored the moment you meet them, even on another player's turn and even after you have passed. You may score several in a turn but only one per combat or step: one in a space combat and another in the ground combat that follows is fine, two from the same combat is not.

### two-players-score-in-same-combat

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Armand7734 · Community · 2020-10-02

Clipper · Community · 2020-10-02

Whichever condition is met first. Destroying a flagship or war sun happens during a combat round, before the combat ends and has a winner. If both trigger at the same moment, the active player goes first.

### what-counts-as-winning-a-combat
Do Space Cannon, Bombardment or landing on an empty planet count as winning a combat for objectives?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

MollyMae · Community · 2024-07-17

hooliganj · Community · 2024-07-17

Pete Walsh · Community · 2020-05-30

dr_worm_md · Community · 2018-03-04

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-03-17

sigmazero13 · Community · 2022-04-16

No — a combat is a space combat or a ground combat, and you win when your units remain and the opponent's don't. Destroying ships with Space Cannon during movement, bombarding away all defenders, or landing unopposed involves no combat, so it can't score 'win a combat' objectives or take Shard of the Throne. A Naalu Foresight escape means no combat happened either. A space dock or PDS alone doesn't make a combat.

### space-cannon-and-direct-hit-for-fleets-to-dust

No to both. Direct Hit's own text destroys the ship, not the Space Cannon, and fighters lost to the capacity rule weren't destroyed by Space Cannon; FFG confirmed both by email. Space Cannon fire resolves player by player, so only the player whose own shots meet the condition scores.

### beat-player-with-most-points

Only if you share the lead: when players are tied for most points, each of them counts as having the most. If you lead alone, nobody else qualifies. The check is made at the time of the combat.

### total-war-variant

Each time you destroy enemy units you take commodities equal to their combined cost and keep them on a planet in your home system; with 10 there you may take the component action for a point. ### no-critical-hits

No — there are no automatic hits or misses. A roll hits when the result, after modifiers, equals or beats the unit's combat value, so with enough bonuses even a 1 can hit.

### combat-only-two-players
Can more than two players fight in the same space combat?

Per the rulebook — Space Combat

phunkysai · Community · 2024-01-12

IncrediSteve · Community · 2024-01-12

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

No — a space combat is always between two players, and it continues until only one player's ships remain or one side retreats. Other players can still have ground forces or structures on planets in that system.

### sustain-damage-basics
How does Sustain Damage work, and is it optional?

Per the rulebook — Space Combat

The rulebook covers this under "Space Combat".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-06

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-06

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-07

Old_Man_gi · Community · 2025-04-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-20

Before hits are assigned, a unit with Sustain Damage may cancel one hit; it is placed on its side as damaged and can't do so again until repaired in the status phase. It is optional: you may skip it and assign the hit to the unit, destroying it. An undamaged dreadnought therefore needs two hits, which can come in different rounds.

### sustain-damage-vs-destroy-effects
Can Sustain Damage save a ship from Exotrireme II, Assault Cannon or other 'destroy' effects?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Jatta Pake · Community · 2017-12-01

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-01

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-01

garfielder · Community · 2017-09-19

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-09-08

csouth154 · Community · 2019-03-06

No — Sustain Damage only cancels hits, and these effects destroy units without producing hits. For Exotrireme II, the Sardakk player chooses which ships (up to 2) are destroyed; it is used after a round of combat, and each Exotrireme II may use it.

### assigned-ability-hits-and-sustain
When Dimensional Splicer or Yin's Devotion hits my ship, who picks the target, and can I use Sustain Damage?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Bonitatem · Community · 2018-05-20

The_Chiprel · Community · 2020-02-26

Jatta Pake · Community · 2017-12-07

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-07

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-24

The Creuss or Yin player chooses which of your ships takes the hit, and assigns it immediately when the ability resolves. Only that ship can use its own Sustain Damage to cancel it; you can't move the hit elsewhere.

### start-of-combat-timing
Are 'start of combat' and 'start of the first combat round' abilities in the same timing window, and how often do they happen?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-03

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-03

Clipper · Community · 2018-04-29

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-04-29

hooliganj · Community · 2026-08-01

Lowecore · Community · 2026-08-01disagrees

Yes — they are the same window, before Anti-Fighter Barrage, and ordered by the usual timing rules. Later rounds only have 'start of a round' windows, so start-of-combat abilities like Assault Cannon happen once per combat. Anything destroyed in that window, such as a destroyer hit by Dimensional Splicer, never fires its barrage.

### assault-cannon-order-and-targets
If both players have start-of-combat abilities like Assault Cannon or Skilled Retreat, which resolves first?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-14

adrim · Community · 2018-12-23

IndyBart · Community · 2021-01-30

hooliganj · Community · 2021-01-30

csouth154 · Community · 2019-09-10

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-04-24

The active player's, then the other player's, with each trigger re-checked when its turn comes. So the attacker's Assault Cannon can push the defender below 3 non-fighter ships and deny the defender's own Assault Cannon, and it destroys a ship before the defender's Skilled Retreat moves the rest away. Assault Cannon is mandatory when it applies and can never destroy a fighter.

### rerolls-after-all-dice
With rerolls or die modifiers like the Hacan flagship or Crown of Thalnos, must I decide before rolling my other ships?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

russe · Community · 2023-03-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-06-09

Clipper · Community · 2020-06-10

Ndjcirnc · Community · 2023-02-17

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-03

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-18superseded

No — since the Codex rules update, rerolls and modifiers are applied after you have rolled all your dice, so you see every result before choosing. The older reading of deciding ship type by ship type is out of date.

### retreat-announcement-rules
Who can retreat from a space combat, and does an announced retreat still happen if the enemy is wiped out?

Community consensus

The rulebook covers who announces first; players agree on the rest, citing the current Living Rules Reference.

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

Clipper · Community · 2018-10-27

MATTtheGM · Community · 2022-04-18

brettellmd · Community · 2022-04-18

Clipper · Community · 2019-01-28

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-11superseded

Either player can announce a retreat, the attacker included, but the defender announces first, and if the defender retreats the attacker can't that round. You can only announce one if you have a legal system to retreat to (so Rout needs one too). If your opponent has no ships left after the round, the combat simply ends and your retreat doesn't happen; answers from 2017 saying you still had to leave predate that rule.

### retreat-destinations
Where can my ships retreat to?

Per the rulebook — Space Combat

The rulebook covers this under "Space Combat".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

DarkThomy · Community · 2021-10-16

McBartsomeville · Community · 2021-10-16

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-09-22

Clipper · Community · 2018-09-22

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-15

To an adjacent system with no other player's ships that contains your units or a planet you control; Dark Energy Tap drops the second requirement. Movement bonuses don't matter, as a retreat always goes to an adjacent system. Under Enforced Travel Ban, alpha and beta wormholes give no adjacency for a retreat. Whether a nebula is a legal destination is disputed; see the nebula question under movement.

### retreat-with-ground-forces
When my ships retreat, can they pick up ground forces from my planets in the combat system?

Community consensus

Players agree the Living Rules Reference v1.2 changed this, superseding the developer's 2018 ruling; no newer official post on BGG.

GrumblingGamer · Community · 2019-06-13

hooliganj · Community · 2019-06-13

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-06-13

Clipper · Community · 2021-01-16

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-10-31superseded

Grunt778083 · Community · 2021-01-16disagrees

Yes, under the current Living Rules Reference: retreating ships move and transport units like any other movement, so they may pick up ground forces from planets there within their capacity. The old rule of lifting ground forces into space when you announce the retreat was removed in v1.2 (some translations still have it), which superseded the developer's 2018 answers about choosing units at announcement.

### units-left-behind-after-retreat
What happens to units that can't go with a retreat?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

McBartsomeville · Community · 2021-10-18

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-07-17

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-07-18

0 hr · Community · 2018-07-18

Padish · Community · 2018-01-11

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-09

All your ships with a move value retreat together. Fighters and ground forces in the space area that the retreating ships can't carry are destroyed, even if a space dock could have supported them. Ground forces on planets are not destroyed; they stay, and the winner may bombard and invade them.

### skilled-retreat-rules
How does Skilled Retreat differ from a normal retreat?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

JamesG · Community · 2018-03-12

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-10-29

harven · Community · 2025-04-16

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-02-28

Marcial · Community · 2019-02-28

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-10disagrees

It isn't a retreat in rules terms but a move, so Intercept can't cancel it (only Sabotage), and the usual transport rules apply: ships can pick up ground forces from planets, and fighters move with the fleet without needing capacity. Capacity and fleet limits apply again at the destination, and you choose which excess units to lose. It can go to any adjacent system without other players' ships, including the one the attacker came from.

### skilled-retreat-own-command-token
Can I retreat or Skilled Retreat into, or carry units out of, systems that contain my command token?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-01-25

Clipper · Community · 2019-03-18

Clipper · Community · 2020-01-21

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-10-30

Clipper · Community · 2022-07-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-01-16

Yes on both counts. A normal retreat or Skilled Retreat may go into a system that already has your command token; you just don't place a second one. And retreating ships can carry units out of the combat system even if your token is there, because the transport restriction excludes the active system, whoever activated it.

### combat-stalemate
What happens if neither side can ever win a space combat?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

russe · Community · 2018-03-07

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-07

pkitty · Community · 2017-12-23

The Living Rules Reference FAQ says the attacker must retreat. If the attacker has nowhere legal to go, players generally treat those ships as destroyed.

### combat-is-mandatory
Can two players agree not to fight when their ships share a system, or not to roll or assign hits?

Per the rulebook — Space Combat

The rulebook covers this under "Space Combat".

zamoose · Community · 2021-07-30

Marcial · Community · 2021-07-30

to_mars · Community · 2021-12-10

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-12-10

hooliganj · Community · 2025-08-15

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

No — when two players have ships in the same space area (or ground forces on the same planet), combat happens and continues until one side is gone; rolling and assigning hits are mandatory and a deal can't override the rules. Retreating, after at least one round, is the only legal way out. Ships and ground forces never fight each other.

### hit-assignment-simultaneous
In space combat, who assigns hits first?

Best reading — unconfirmed

One experienced player's reading; no official ruling or wider agreement.

zamoose · Community · 2023-02-22

zamoose · Community · 2023-02-22

Nobody first — both players assign hits in the same step. Assigning hits isn't an ability, so no initiative order applies; cancelling hits, as with Sustain Damage, is.

### two-hits-on-sustain-unit
Can a unit with Sustain Damage be destroyed by two hits in one round?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2019-09-29

Clipper · Community · 2023-03-11

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-07

Truji · Community · 2025-12-28

TomKyrre · Community · 2025-12-28

Yes — Sustain Damage cancels one hit, not every hit. If you must take more hits than you can cancel and that unit is what's left, the next hit destroys it; it is not a second hit point. Non-Euclidean Shielding lets it cancel two.

### capacity-during-combat
If my carrier is destroyed in combat, are the fighters and ground forces it carried destroyed at once?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2024-10-16

McBartsomeville · Community · 2022-10-16

Dubledox · Community · 2022-10-17

Weltenreiter · Community · 2021-04-18

donmakaron · Community · 2021-04-18

zamoose · Community · 2018-11-28

No — capacity isn't checked during combat, so they stay while it lasts. When combat ends, each player checks capacity and removes fighters and ground forces in space that are no longer supported. If all your ships are destroyed, the units they carried go with them. If the side left in space has only fighters it can no longer support, they are removed and the combat counts as a draw.

### after-skilled-retreat-attacker-continues
If the defender leaves with Skilled Retreat or Foresight, does the attacker still invade, and has anyone won a combat?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2020-01-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-01-06

hooliganj · Community · 2025-05-31

Bigland · Community · 2017-11-23

McBartsomeville · Community · 2023-10-03

MollyMae · Community · 2018-08-20

The attacker carries on to bombardment, invasion and production as normal. But no combat was won: after a normal retreat the player left in the system wins, whereas Foresight happens before combat and Skilled Retreat leaves no winner, so 'win a combat' objectives and effects don't trigger. The destination system isn't activated, so nobody fires Space Cannon or invades there.

### failed-invasion-ships-stay
What happens to the attacker's ships if the invasion fails?

Per the rulebook — Producing Units

The rulebook covers this under "Producing Units".

ifaeb · Community · 2024-01-13

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Producing Units

They stay in the system; nothing makes them leave. While they remain, the defender's space dock there is blockaded and can only produce ground forces.

### space-cannon-wipes-attacker-no-combat
If Space Cannon Offense destroys all of the active player's ships, did a space combat take place?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

csouth154 · Community · 2019-04-21

Clipper · Community · 2019-04-21

No — Space Cannon Offense happens at the end of movement, outside space combat. If it destroys every ship, no combat occurs and 'win a combat' effects don't apply.

### what-is-a-combat-roll
Do Morale Boost, faction modifiers and rerolls affect Anti-Fighter Barrage, Space Cannon, Bombardment or Ambush?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-02-03

Nurgle84 · Community · 2021-02-04

hooliganj · Community · 2017-11-17

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-01-07

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-06-27

Surge72 · Community · 2020-12-09

No — only rolls made against a unit's combat value are combat rolls. Anti-Fighter Barrage, Space Cannon, Bombardment, Mentak's Ambush and Sardakk's Courageous to the End are ability rolls, so Morale Boost, Sardakk's +1, Jol-Nar's Fragile and effects that reroll combat rolls don't touch them. Anti-Fighter Barrage happens only in the first round.

### rolling-dice-by-combat-value
Can I roll all my combat dice together when my ships have different combat values?

Per the rulebook — Space Combat

The rulebook covers this under "Space Combat".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Space Combat

Chigatterun · Community · 2020-08-13

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-08-13

JaHeit · Community · 2021-01-06

Only for ships with the same combat value; ships with different values roll separately, so each die stays with the unit that rolled it. Modifiers are added to the die result, so +1 makes a hit easier.

### retreating-ships-still-roll

Yes — announcing a retreat doesn't stop you firing; you roll normally and then leave in the retreat step. Retreating in that step also escapes Exotrireme II, which acts at the end of the round.

### retreated-ground-forces-cannot-land

### invade-several-planets-one-action
Can I invade several planets in the same system in one tactical action?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Yes — in the invasion step you commit ground forces to any or all planets in the active system, declaring which go where. Each ground force lands on only one planet, so two planets need at least two ground forces. Four infantry arriving at an empty two-planet system can put two on each and take both.

### landing-only-in-invasion-step

No — ground forces land on planets only by being committed in the invasion step of a tactical action, so you must activate the system even to drop infantry on your own planet. Movement abilities used as component actions skip the tactical action steps and can't land anyone.

### fighters-cannot-invade

Falcone1983 · Community · 2017-11-22

Fighters can't invade (barring specific faction abilities), and ships take part only through abilities like Bombardment. You need ground forces in the active system's space area to commit.

### ground-combat-needs-both-sides
Is there a ground combat if Bombardment or Space Cannon Defense wipes out one side first?

Per the rulebook — Invasion and Ground Combat

The rulebook covers this under "Invasion and Ground Combat".

ajmarshall · Community · 2020-05-09

ajmarshall · Community · 2019-10-21

donmakaron · Community · 2019-10-03

Clipper · Community · 2019-10-03

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Invasion and Ground Combat

No — ground combat only happens if both players still have ground forces on the planet after those steps. If Bombardment kills every defender, you land unopposed; if Space Cannon Defense kills every invader, nobody lands. Structures don't count as defenders, and start-of-combat abilities such as Yin's Indoctrination can't be used without a combat.

### space-cannon-offense-without-moving
Does Space Cannon Offense happen if the active player moves nothing into the active system?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Marcial · Community · 2024-03-04

Yes — it is a step of every tactical action, not something movement triggers, so enemy Space Cannon can hit the active player's ships already in the system. Equally, you may activate a system purely to fire your own Space Cannon into it. You must finish moving before anyone fires, so you can't see the results first.

### space-cannon-offense-targets
During Space Cannon Offense, who can shoot at whom?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

csouth154 · Community · 2019-10-02

Crikrunner · Community · 2017-11-27disagrees

The active player fires first and chooses which other player's ships in the active system take the hits. Then each other player with Space Cannon in range fires, but only at the active player's ships — a third party can't shoot the defender or help one side of someone else's battle.

### sustain-damage-vs-space-cannon

### experimental-battlestation-trigger
When can Experimental Battlestation be used, and what can it shoot?

Designer ruling

The game's developer answered this directly on BGG in 2018.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-11-14

Clipper · Community · 2018-11-14

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-11-15

0 hr · Community · 2018-11-14superseded

Only when another player's ships actually move during the movement step of a tactical action: not on retreats, not when nothing moves in, and never on your own turn as active player. It fires as part of Space Cannon Offense and can target only the active player's ships in the active system. An earlier emailed ruling allowing any movement, retreats included, was walked back by the developer.

### space-cannon-only-active-system
Can Space Cannon fire at ships outside the active system, such as ships passing by, retreating, or in a neighboring system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

toskk · Community · 2025-11-20

IncrediSteve · Community · 2026-03-28

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-05-06

No — Space Cannon only ever targets the active system: Offense after movement, Defense during invasion. PDS II's Deep Space Cannon and the Xxcha flagship let a unit in an adjacent system fire into the active system, never out of it. Ships passing through systems in range or retreating into one aren't shot at. A unit with the ability that moved into the active system fires as normal.

### space-cannon-one-player-at-a-time
When several players fire Space Cannon Offense, are they resolved together?

No — one player at a time, starting with the active player and then the others in order. Each player's roll, hit assignment, Sustain Damage and responses like Direct Hit finish before the next fires, so a unit destroyed earlier, such as an Xxcha flagship, never gets to shoot.

### space-cannon-through-wormholes
Can Space Cannon with range reach through a wormhole?

Yes — wormhole adjacency counts for Space Cannon range, including the delta wormhole on the Creuss flagship, and even when a law turns off wormholes for movement. The Nomad flagship's mech adjacency is not a wormhole, so it doesn't extend Space Cannon.

### bombardment-optional

The Bombardment step always happens, but using the ability is optional: the active player may bombard. You can also bombard with no ground forces to land and simply not take the planet, since committing ground forces is optional too.

### plasma-scoring-one-die
How many extra dice does Plasma Scoring add to Bombardment and Space Cannon?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

MATTPROV3 · Community · 2018-02-11

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-11

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-27

csouth154 · Community · 2019-06-02

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-11disagrees

One extra die per use of the ability, not per unit or per planet. Bombardment is a single step, so one extra die however many planets you bombard. Space Cannon Offense rolls one die per unit in range plus one from Plasma Scoring. Each planet's Space Cannon Defense is a separate roll, so each can get the extra die.

### who-fires-space-cannon-offense
Can the active player fire their own Space Cannon, including PDS II in an adjacent system, into the system they activate?

Yes — every player with Space Cannon in range of the active system may fire, the active player first. You can activate a system purely so your PDS II next door can shoot enemy ships there, even a supernova if someone like Muaat activated it. Fighters in the space area are valid targets; ground forces in space and Saar Floating Factories are not.

### space-cannon-defense-which-pds

Only PDS on the planet being invaded. A PDS on another planet in the system, or a PDS II in an adjacent system, doesn't fire then. A PDS can fire Space Cannon Offense and later Space Cannon Defense in the same tactical action.

### bombardment-assignment
Can one ship bombard several planets, or split its dice between them?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

bearsfan1978 · Community · 2020-11-17

zamoose · Community · 2019-09-09

No — there is one Bombardment step per invasion, and before rolling you declare which planet each unit bombards; a unit bombards a single planet with all its dice. You may pick any planet in the system, since bombardment comes before ground forces are committed. A war sun removes Planetary Shield from other players' units in its system, so all your bombarding units can then hit planets with PDS.

### space-cannon-fires-once
How many times does a PDS fire in one tactical action?

Once at Space Cannon Offense, after movement, however many ships came in, plus once more at Space Cannon Defense if ground forces are committed to its planet. PDS II improves range and combat value, not the number of shots. A PDS needs no ground forces with it to fire, and having your command token in its system doesn't stop it.

### ceasefire-and-disable-vs-space-cannon
Do Ceasefire or Disable stop Space Cannon?

Ceasefire only stops ships moving in, so Space Cannon, Bombardment, invasion and production all still happen. Disable is played at invasion and only shuts off PDS on the planets being invaded; Space Cannon Offense has already been fired by then.

### ships-must-end-in-active-system
Can I move ships partway toward the active system, or move ships that can't reach it?

Per the rulebook — Movement Details
