Jatta Pake · Community · 2017-11-21

j76goatboy · Community · 2017-11-21

No — every ship that moves must end its move in the active system, along a path no longer than its move value. A ship that can't get there doesn't move at all, so you can't stop short, for example to set up Space Cannon.

### move-out-and-back-into-active-system
Can a ship leave the active system and come back in the same move, for example to fetch ground forces?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

brisingre · Community · 2017-08-18

Clipper · Community · 2019-01-13

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-08

Yes — a ship that starts in the active system may move out and back in if its move value allows, and a ship may pass back through a system it already crossed. None of the systems it leaves or starts from may hold your command token, apart from the active system, and enemy ships still block the path.

### what-blocks-movement
What blocks my ships from moving through a system?

Per the rulebook — Movement Details

The rulebook covers this under "Movement Details".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Movement Details

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-10-03

Clipper · Community · 2019-01-31

Skootur · Community · 2017-10-16

gpoirier · Community · 2025-09-11

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-18

Any other player's ships in it, fighters included. The Living Rules Reference dropped the old exception for fighters, so even Fighter I next to a space dock blocks. Your own command tokens don't block passing through, and neither do enemy ground forces or PDS. A deal can't let you through; only effects like Light/Wave Deflector, In the Silence of Space or the Yssaril flagship do.

### moving-into-system-with-own-token-later
Can I move more ships into a system that already has my command token on a later turn?

No — ships only move into the active system, which stops being active when that tactical action ends, and you can't activate a system holding your token. A ship with move 1 can't reach a system two away in one action either; Warfare removes a token but doesn't extend movement.

### moving-ships-simultaneous
Do ships move one at a time, and can I wait to see gravity rift rolls before moving others?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

No — you declare every moving ship and they move together; rift rolls come after. Fleet pool isn't checked in systems you pass through, only at the end, and you remove any excess then. The Creuss flagship is the exception: it may move before or after your other ships, but not in the middle of their moves.

### gravity-rift-bonus-and-roll
How do gravity rifts work: when do I get +1 move and when do I roll?

Per the rulebook — Movement Details

The rulebook covers this under "Movement Details".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Movement Details

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-10-19

Clipper · Community · 2019-04-11

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-10-19

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-15

jboot8 · Community · 2021-04-18

A ship whose path moves out of or through a rift gets +1 move for that path, one per rift, even if it could otherwise only just reach the rift. Each ship that leaves or passes through a rift rolls, and is removed on a 1-3. A ship that stops in a rift gains nothing and doesn't roll until it later leaves. Trace the whole path first, then check it against the boosted move value.

### gravity-rift-transported-units
When a carrier leaves a gravity rift, do the fighters and ground forces it carries roll too?

Clipper · Community · 2021-07-31

MrDeadfisch · Community · 2017-11-26disagrees

No — only the ships moving out roll. Declare which ship carries what before rolling; if a carrier is lost, everything it carries goes with it, and other carriers' spare capacity can't save them. Units lost this way are removed rather than destroyed, so effects like Infantry II don't trigger.

### nebula-movement
How do nebulae work for movement, and does Gravity Drive help?

A ship may only enter a nebula if it is the active system, so it can never pass through one. A ship leaving a nebula has move 1, and bonuses like Gravity Drive or Flank Speed are then added on top.

### retreat-into-nebula
Can ships retreat or Skilled Retreat into a nebula?

### supernova-and-muaat
Who can move into or through a supernova?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Nobody normally. Muaat's faction ability lets their ships move through supernovas; Magmus Reactor lets ships move into and stop in one. ### anomalies-restrict-moving-in-only
If I lose Antimass Deflectors while my ships sit in an asteroid field, or place units in an anomaly, what happens?

Nothing — anomaly rules restrict moving into or through them, not being in them or moving out. Ships already there stay and may leave normally, and effects can place units in anomalies.

### which-systems-are-anomalies
Which systems count as anomalies?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-23

mpappy · Community · 2022-05-04

Dirzuga · Community · 2022-05-04

Only tiles with a red border: asteroid fields, nebulae, gravity rifts and supernovas. Empty systems and wormhole systems are not anomalies unless bordered. Antimass Deflectors is the technology that lets ships move into and through asteroid fields.

### wormholes-are-optional-adjacency
How do wormholes work, and must my ships use them?

Per the rulebook — Movement Details

The rulebook covers this under "Movement Details".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Movement Details

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-06-09

hooliganj · Community · 2017-10-02

TwoAMArts · Community · 2023-08-22

csouth154 · Community · 2019-10-14

PistonLoop · Community · 2022-07-02

Systems with matching wormholes are adjacent for all purposes, movement, Space Cannon and neighbors included. A wormhole only adds connections: you don't have to use it, and a wormhole system can be activated and moved through like any other. Being in one wormhole system doesn't put you next to the systems around the matching one.

### creuss-wormholes-usable-by-all
Can other players use the Creuss Gate and the Creuss flagship's delta wormhole?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-11-17

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-24

Yes — delta wormholes follow normal rules, so anyone can travel between the Creuss Gate, the Creuss home system and the flagship's system. Since the flagship's system contains a Creuss ship, enemies need a way to move through it (such as Light/Wave Deflector) or must stop and fight there.

truephoenyx · Community · 2017-08-30

CBpegasus · Community · 2017-08-30

HP12C_guy · Community · 2023-08-18

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-17

Its system counts as containing a delta wormhole. If it moves into the active system first, that system becomes adjacent to other delta wormholes such as the Creuss home system, so your other ships can come in through it; or it can move after them. It can also use its own wormhole, for example to go home. If it moves first through a gravity rift and is destroyed, the wormhole is gone before the others move.

For the Creuss only, systems with alpha wormholes and systems with beta wormholes are adjacent to each other; no game effect, Enforced Travel Ban included, can stop that. Other players can't fire Space Cannon or move through that connection.

### movement-bonuses-stack

### saar-floating-factory-movement-effects
Do movement effects like Gravity Drive and gravity rifts apply to Saar Floating Factories?

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-16

Yes — the developer ruled that anything affecting movement affects Floating Factories and the ships moving with them, so they get Gravity Drive, Flank Speed and rift bonuses, and he later confirmed they roll when leaving a gravity rift. ### ability-movement-rules

Only the ability's own text applies: move values, the command-token restriction and other tactical-action movement rules are ignored, and the moving ships can transport units that start with them. Anomaly rules still apply, so no ability moves ships into a supernova unless it says so.

### moving-ground-forces-between-planets

### system-not-adjacent-to-itself
Is a system adjacent to itself, and are planets in the same system adjacent?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Johnnymaxx · Community · 2017-11-27

DrewDecimal · Community · 2017-11-27

Pete Walsh · Community · 2020-08-08

Morix_Jak · Community · 2020-08-08

Clipper · Community · 2021-02-07

IncrediSteve · Community · 2024-03-19

A system is never adjacent to itself, so being in a system doesn't count as adjacent to it. Planets are treated as adjacent to other planets in their system and in neighboring systems, which matters for effects like Xxcha's Peace Accords.

### fleet-pool-when-checked
When is the fleet pool limit checked, and can I bring extra ships into a fight and trim afterwards?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-11-09

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-22

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-24

Clipper · Community · 2021-01-17

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-03-25

At all times, except for ships that are only moving through other systems. Once ships arrive in the active system, any non-fighter ships over your fleet pool are removed straight away, before Space Cannon Offense and combat, so you can't use extras to soak hits.

### exceeding-fleet-pool-deliberately
Can I deliberately go over my fleet pool by moving or producing, then remove ships of my choice?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-12-31

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-09-28

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-24

garfielder · Community · 2017-09-28

Strategery21 · Community · 2024-01-08

JamesG · Community · 2017-10-10

Yes — nothing stops you; you just remove the excess right away, choosing which. That lets you, say, build a new ship in a full system and scrap an old one, or move extra carriers in to deliver ground forces. Production itself isn't capped by the fleet pool; it is checked once the units are placed.

### fleet-pool-reduction-destroyed
If an effect cuts my fleet pool, like Neuroglaive or Fleet Regulations, what happens to the excess ships, and does anyone get credit for them?

Clipper · Community · 2018-04-08

Clipper · Community · 2018-06-18

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-06-18

You lose the token at once and must bring every system back within the new limit right away. Ships removed this way are destroyed, but no player destroyed them, so 'when you destroy' effects and objectives don't trigger for whoever caused it. Neuroglaive can even drop a fleet pool to zero.

### capacity-what-it-covers

Per the rulebook — Fleet Pool, Capacity, and Transport

gpoirier · Community · 2025-08-16

Capacity holds fighters and ground forces in the space area, in any mix: Cruiser II's or a dreadnought's capacity can hold fighters too. Any unit with a capacity value can transport, including some flagships, war suns and faction units; check the faction sheet. Ground forces on planets need no capacity.

### space-dock-fighter-allowance

### fighters-without-capacity

Not normal fighters: any beyond the capacity of your ships and space docks there are removed. ### transport-pickup-and-dropoff
Where can a moving ship pick up and drop off units?

Per the rulebook — Fleet Pool, Capacity, and Transport

The rulebook covers this under "Fleet Pool, Capacity, and Transport".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Fleet Pool, Capacity, and Transport

AgentDrake · Community · 2018-08-19

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-25

MollyMae · Community · 2019-01-12

Benj_ · Community · 2018-09-26

TerTer · Community · 2020-04-13

It can pick up fighters and ground forces in the system it starts in, every system it passes through and the active system, but not from any other system with your command token. Units come off only in the active system, after the ship finishes moving; it can't drop a load and go back for more. Fighters that don't fit stay behind.

### over-capacity-to-destroy-own-units
Can a carrier pick up more units than its capacity so the excess are destroyed, or leave fighters behind to die?

Designer ruling

The game's developer answered this directly on BGG in 2018.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-10-31

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-10-31

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-10-30

Soul_ · Community · 2018-10-29

0 hr · Community · 2018-10-31superseded

You can't pick up more than the moving ships' capacity, so you can't overload to destroy your own units. But you can load ground forces and leave fighters or infantry already in space behind; capacity is a system total, so those left over are removed if what remains can't hold them.

“You could not, however, attempt to transport more units than the moving ships have capacity for in an attempt to destroy the excess.”

— CreussEmissary, designer

### units-not-tied-to-ships-after-moving
After ships stop moving, are fighters and ground forces still tied to the ship that carried them?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-03-22

TinTeardrop · Community · 2020-01-26

THLY · Community · 2019-09-24

THLY · Community · 2021-12-14

afrag · Community · 2019-09-05disagrees

No — only while moving (and when leaving a gravity rift). Afterwards only the total capacity in the system matters, so if a carrier is lost, the cargo survives if other ships have room, and you choose which excess units to remove. Units removed for lack of capacity are removed, not destroyed, so Infantry II doesn't trigger.

### capacity-checked-outside-combat
When is capacity checked if a carrier is lost?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

csouth154 · Community · 2019-09-10

Clipper · Community · 2019-06-06

hooliganj · Community · 2017-11-27

k8rthik · Community · 2026-01-06

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-06-23

Immediately whenever it isn't combat, for example after each player's Space Cannon Offense volley, before the next player fires. During combat fighters and ground forces may exceed capacity and keep fighting; the excess is removed when the space combat ends, before invasion. So if all your ships die, their ground forces can't invade.

### who-can-transact-and-when
Who can make transactions, and when?

Per the rulebook — The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

The rulebook covers this under "The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

IncrediSteve · Community · 2023-02-22

GAFBlizzard · Community · 2023-02-22

During the action phase, only the active player can transact, and only with neighbors: one transaction with each neighbor per turn. A transaction trades any trade goods and commodities plus at most one promissory note. Deals that don't exchange components can be made with anyone at any time.

### one-transaction-per-turn-not-round
Is the one-transaction limit per turn or per round?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-12-03

Clipper · Community · 2020-12-03

Per turn. A round covers all four phases; a turn is one player's turn in the action phase. So two neighbors can trade on one player's turn and again on the other's.

### agenda-phase-transactions-non-neighbors
Can players make transactions with non-neighbors during the agenda phase?

Designer ruling

The rulebook allows it, and the developer confirmed on BGG in 2019 that it doesn't make players neighbors.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2019-01-17

hooliganj · Community · 2019-01-17

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-12-05

Clipper · Community · 2018-12-03

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-08

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · 4. Agenda Phase

Yes — while each agenda is being resolved, from its reveal until its outcome resolves, every pair of players may make one transaction, neighbors or not. That doesn't make them neighbors for anything else, as the developer confirmed when asked about Mentak's Pillage.

### trading-action-cards
Can players trade action cards?

Per the rulebook — The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

The rulebook covers this under "The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities".

AgentDrake · Community · 2026-01-04

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

No — transactions cover only trade goods, commodities and promissory notes. The Hacan are the exception: their faction ability lets them include action cards, still within the one-transaction-per-player limit.

### when-is-a-deal-binding
When is a deal binding?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Duskwalker · Community · 2017-08-24

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-08-25

Sande24 · Community · 2017-08-25

IncrediSteve · Community · 2023-02-22

pkitty · Community · 2023-02-22

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-08-25disagrees

Only when its terms resolve immediately — for example, taking a trade good not to fire Space Cannon at the moment you decide whether to fire binds you not to fire. Promises about future actions, and if-then conditions, are non-binding and can be broken. You can bind yourself to an act, but not to its result, such as a card that might be Sabotaged.

### vote-deals-binding
Is a deal to buy someone's votes binding?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2020-10-25

Dulkal · Community · 2020-10-25

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-16

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-04

Xelvonar · Community · 2018-02-04

Only for a player about to cast votes right now. Paying the next voter in exchange for their vote binds it, since nothing can happen in between; a promise to vote a certain way later can still be broken.

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

rubberchicken · Community · 2024-01-12

### what-can-be-traded
What can be exchanged in a transaction?

Per the rulebook — The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

The rulebook covers this under "The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

Clipper · Community · 2021-06-04

zamoose · Community · 2024-03-09

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-11-15

Duskwalker · Community · 2019-01-31

CBpegasus · Community · 2017-09-09

Any number of trade goods and commodities, plus one promissory note from each side, and relic fragments with Prophecy of Kings. Technologies, units, planets, laws, command tokens, the speaker token and secret objectives can't be traded; action cards only when the Hacan are one of the parties, in any number and either direction. A transaction may be lopsided, even a gift.

### transaction-not-an-action
Is a transaction an action, and when during a turn can it happen?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-12-21

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-02-11

Sande24 · Community · 2017-11-27

It isn't an action — the active player can make one with each neighbor at any point in their turn, even mid-combat or between the parts of a strategy card, and still take their action. Either side may propose it, and it counts against the active player's limit with that player. Two non-active players can't transact with each other.

### no-transactions-strategy-status
Can players make transactions in the strategy or status phase?

No — transactions happen only in the action phase (active player with neighbors) and the agenda phase (anyone). Players can still talk and make non-binding deals at any time, even during galaxy setup.

### who-are-neighbors
Who counts as my neighbor?

Per the rulebook — The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

The rulebook covers this under "The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · The Economy: Resources, Trade Goods, and Commodities

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-08-26

hooliganj · Community · 2024-10-14

Clipper · Community · 2020-02-11

hooliganj · Community · 2021-05-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-10-15

Players with a unit or controlled planet in the same system or adjacent systems, wormholes included, so two players fighting in a system are neighbors. Ships moving through systems don't create neighbors on the way, since they are never in those systems. The Hacan can transact with non-neighbors, and anyone can with them, but that doesn't make them neighbors for Pillage.

### commodities-become-trade-goods
When do commodities turn into trade goods, and can I give them and get them back in one transaction?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-20

Clipper · Community · 2021-02-16

hooliganj · Community · 2025-10-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-11-14

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-14

greendayfan333 · Community · 2018-03-12

A commodity becomes a trade good the moment another player receives it, whether by transaction, Pillage or another effect. A transaction is one simultaneous exchange, so commodities you receive can't be sent back in it; that would be a second transaction, which must wait for another turn. You can only give what you actually have.

### trading-received-promissory-notes

CBpegasus · Community · 2017-12-18

Yes — any note in your hand can be traded on, and you needn't show it, though the recipient may want to see it first. If the deal named a specific note, you must hand over that one. Notes face up in your play area, like Support for the Throne, can't be traded or handed back voluntarily.

### trade-strategy-replenish-not-transaction

### deal-not-to-attack
Is a deal like 'take these trade goods and don't attack me this turn' binding?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-04

oatesatm · Community · 2018-01-04

Sande24 · Community · 2018-01-05

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-13

Simboubou · Community · 2017-11-28

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-05disagrees

No — a promise covering a period of time can't be resolved immediately, so it can be broken. To make something binding, pay for a specific immediate act, such as choosing not to fire Space Cannon right now, or trade for a promissory note like Ceasefire. A deal for future payment is non-binding too. Deals can never override rules like mandatory combat rolls.

### three-way-transactions

Only as two separate transactions, one with each, and since you get one transaction per neighbor per turn, part of it will rest on a non-binding promise.

### military-support-once-per-sol-turn

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

chrisdk · Community · 2023-06-13

At most once per Sol turn: it is used at the start of Sol's turn and then returns to Sol. If Sol trades it out again that turn, the start of that turn has passed, so it waits until Sol's next turn.

### space-dock-production-value

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Pete Walsh · Community · 2018-01-15

A Space Dock I has Production equal to its planet's resource value plus 2 (plus 4 with Space Dock II), so on a 3-resource planet it builds up to 5 units. The planet being exhausted doesn't matter.

### production-limits-units-not-cost
Does Production limit the number of units or their total cost?

Community consensus

Clipper · Community · 2020-06-07

Clipper · Community · 2022-01-30

The number of units. Each fighter or infantry counts as one unit, even though two cost 1 resource, so a war sun uses one point of Production and you pay its 12 cost separately. The value isn't used up: you can produce up to it every time you get a chance to produce there.

jeffwagner · Community · 2017-08-29

halbower · Community · 2017-08-28disagrees

Yes — all your Production in the active system is pooled into one production: two space docks of 4 and 3 give 7, and two Arborec infantry with Production 1 can build 2 infantry for 1 resource, even on different planets. You work out the total before producing, so units produced in that step can't add their own Production to it.

### where-produced-ground-forces-go
Where are produced ground forces placed?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

tommygunn2011 · Community · 2018-08-26

Clipper · Community · 2020-05-29

ajmarshall · Community · 2020-05-17

TerTer · Community · 2020-05-29disagrees

On a planet that holds a unit that used Production, never on other planets in the system. With several producing planets you may split them among those planets however you like, even all on one. If the producing unit is in space, like a Saar Floating Factory or Letani Warriors aboard ships, they can go in that space area or on any planet you control in the system.

### produce-only-in-active-system

### blockade-production
Can I produce ships at a space dock when enemy ships are in that system?

Per the rulebook — Producing Units

The rulebook covers this under "Producing Units".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Producing Units

Mordenthral · Community · 2017-12-11

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-11

Lowecore · Community · 2023-04-03

halbower · Community · 2020-07-04

No — a dock (or other Production unit) in a system with another player's ships and none of yours is blockaded and can only produce ground forces. You keep the planet, and your PDS still fire. If Space Cannon or combat clears the enemy ships before the production step, you produce normally. Ground forces alone, without ships, never blockade.

### produce-vs-place

No to both. An effect that says 'produce' means you pay the cost unless it says otherwise. 'Place' or 'gain' is free and isn't producing, so Muaat's Star Forge doesn't count as producing and doesn't trigger Magmus Reactor's trade good.

### sarween-tools-rules
How does Sarween Tools work?

It cuts the combined cost of one use of Production by 1, not 1 per unit or per dock, and can take it to zero, so 2 infantry or 2 fighters can be free. ### magen-defense-grid-disabled-pds
Does the Codex (Omega) Magen Defense Grid still work if the PDS on the planet has been hit by Disable?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2026-04-15

GAFBlizzard · Community · 2026-04-15

haeberich · Community · 2020-06-07disagrees

Yes — Disable only strips the PDS's Planetary Shield and Space Cannon; it is still a structure, so Magen Defense Grid still produces its hit. That hit lands at the start of ground combat, before dice are rolled, so the destroyed unit never rolls. With only structures on the planet there's no ground combat and Magen can't trigger.

### graviton-laser-system-space-cannon-defense
Does Graviton Laser System do anything during Space Cannon Defense?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2025-09-18

Falkman · Community · 2025-09-17

No — you may exhaust it, but the only targets during Space Cannon Defense are the invading ground forces, so there are never non-fighter ships to force hits onto.

### duranium-armor-rules
How does Duranium Armor work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2023-03-25

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-01

Clipper · Community · 2020-05-22

stugal · Community · 2019-09-05

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-12-19

hooliganj · Community · 2018-01-03

Once per combat round, after hits are assigned, it repairs one of your damaged units in that combat that did not use Sustain Damage that round, even if you had no hits to assign. Only units with Sustain Damage can be damaged, so only they benefit: ships in space combat, mechs in ground combat, never units elsewhere on the board. It isn't an extra hit point: a damaged unit that takes another hit is destroyed first. A unit damaged by Space Cannon before combat can be repaired at the end of round one.

Yes — it needs the opponent to produce hits, and they did; cancelling them afterwards doesn't undo that.

It makes Space Cannon hits be assigned to non-fighter ships when possible. They are still ordinary hits, so Sustain Damage can cancel them before assignment. It doesn't affect fighters or ground forces in ground combat, Naalu fighters included.

### technology-specialty-rules

### prerequisites-and-exhausted-tech
Does a technology I just researched, or one that is exhausted, count as a prerequisite?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-05-19

Yes to both. Owned technologies always count, exhausted or not, so the first technology from the Technology primary counts for the second, and you can exhaust AI Development Algorithm and still count it. A coloured faction technology counts as that colour for prerequisites and objectives.

### unit-upgrades-are-technologies
How do unit upgrades work?

Per the rulebook — Technology

The rulebook covers this under "Technology".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Technology

HedleyP · Community · 2025-08-19

Clipper · Community · 2020-03-16

Weltenreiter · Community · 2019-03-05

Clavos · Community · 2026-01-06

Clipper · Community · 2020-12-16

They are technologies you research normally, prerequisites and all; Inheritance Systems can skip their prerequisites like any other. Once gained, lay the card over that unit on your faction sheet; all your units of that type use the new stats right away. Upgrades have no colour, so they don't count as prerequisites or for colour objectives.

Only if the effect says so, as the Technology card does. You still meet prerequisites (specialties can help). 'Gain' is different from 'research': gaining ignores prerequisites and doesn't trigger 'when you research' effects.

Yes. Research Fleet Logistics with the Technology primary on your turn, and once that strategic action, secondaries included, is done you may take a second action that turn; gaining it on another player's turn doesn't give an extra action then. Starting technologies work from the first turn.

### fleet-logistics
What does Fleet Logistics allow?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

On each turn you may take two actions instead of one, of any type, even the same type, such as two tactical actions on two systems. You can't reuse an exhausted strategy card or a discarded action card, and the second action is optional. If the first is Warfare, other players resolve its secondary before your second action.

### infantry-ii-on-the-card

### magen-defense-grid-versions-war-sun

Only the original version, which needs a unit with Planetary Shield: a war sun makes other players' units lose Planetary Shield. It only acts in the first round of ground combat.

### integrated-economy

### inheritance-systems

When you produce in a system that contains a war sun (anyone's) or is adjacent to a supernova; at most one per production. ### keeping-control-of-planets
Do I lose control of a planet if my ground forces leave or are destroyed?

No — you control a planet while you hold its card; with no units there, mark it with a control token. You only lose control when another player gains it, by invading or an effect that says so. That includes home planets you start with, and control never reverts to a former owner automatically.

### home-planets-controlled-at-start
Do I control both planets in my home system at the start, and does that trigger 'gain control' effects?

You control every home planet from the start, empty or not; a control token on an empty one is just a reminder. Because you begin with control rather than gaining it, abilities like Saar's Scavenge don't trigger at setup.

### taking-control-needs-ground-forces
How do I take control of a planet?

Per the rulebook — Tactical Action

The rulebook covers this under "Tactical Action".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Tactical Action

Owen134866 · Community · 2023-05-02

isterbat · Community · 2023-07-11

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-07-03

Nyarlathotep90 · Community · 2018-01-21

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-12-08

Normally by landing at least one ground force on it in the invasion step; ships in the system, or fighters, even with the Naalu flagship, never take a planet. Each planet is taken separately, so one invasion can't claim a second planet you didn't land on. A few effects grant control directly, and say so, like Xxcha's Peace Accords.

hooliganj · Community · 2019-01-29

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-01-30

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Invasion and Ground Combat

No — each planet has its own ground combat, and you take only the planets where your ground forces remain and the defender's don't. If both sides lose all their ground forces on a planet, the defender keeps control.

### different-players-planets-same-system
Can different players control different planets in the same system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-09

afrag · Community · 2017-12-17

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-19

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-19

hooliganj · Community · 2025-11-19

Yes — each planet is controlled separately and nobody controls the space area. Only ships can't share the space area without combat. If a planet's controller has no units on it while someone else's do, control stays put until that player invades.

### structures-destroyed-when-planet-taken
What happens to space docks and PDS when an enemy takes the planet?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Old_Man_gi · Community · 2020-12-21

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-03

Crikrunner · Community · 2017-11-25

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-09-17

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-09-13

Clipper · Community · 2018-09-14disagrees

They are destroyed when another player's ground forces take control of the planet with none of the owner's ground forces left. Structures never fight or block an invasion. Fighters fighting via the Naalu flagship don't destroy them, since they aren't on the planet afterwards. L1Z1X's Assimilate replaces them instead, and the new dock can produce that same action.

### structures-cannot-move
Can PDS or space docks be moved or transported?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2020-01-21

halbower · Community · 2020-02-15

pfischer · Community · 2018-01-14

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-08-16

No — structures stay where they are placed, except the Saar Floating Factories, which move as if they were ships.

### custodians-token-mecatol
How do I take Mecatol Rex, and does the custodians point ever change hands?

Per the rulebook — Control, Structures, and the Custodians Token

The rulebook covers this under "Control, Structures, and the Custodians Token".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Control, Structures, and the Custodians Token

donmakaron · Community · 2022-05-19

hooliganj · Community · 2017-11-28

salsaVerdeguel · Community · 2025-12-28

AgentDrake · Community · 2022-01-18

pfischer · Community · 2017-12-18

Activate it like any system; during invasion, before committing ground forces, spend 6 influence to remove the custodians token, gaining it and 1 victory point, then land. Only the first player to do this gets the point, and it is never lost; later control of Mecatol Rex scores only through Imperial.

### destroying-ground-forces-not-control
If I kill every ground force on a planet with X-89 or Orbital Drop elsewhere, do I take the planet?

No — destroying defenders never changes control, and Orbital Drop only reinforces planets you already control. You must invade in a tactical action to take it.

### saar-dock-not-blockadable-objective

### same-name-action-cards-one-window
Can I play two copies of the same action card, like Morale Boost or Flank Speed, in one timing window?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-06-27

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-08-07

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-01-02

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-08-04

Clipper · Community · 2022-06-08

No — you cannot play more than one action card with the same name in a single timing window to affect the same units or effect. Cards with different names can all be played. Direct Hit can be played more than once because each copy targets a different ship.

### replay-card-after-sabotage
If my action card is cancelled by Sabotage, can I play another copy of the same card in that window?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-06-07

Clipper · Community · 2022-06-08

chrisdk · Community · 2023-04-02

jeffwagner · Community · 2018-10-06disagrees

Yes — a cancelled card is treated as never having been played, so the same-name restriction doesn't stop a second copy. Likewise, if your Sabotage is itself cancelled (by Instinct Training, say), you may play another Sabotage on the original card. Some older answers said a second copy was not allowed.

### third-party-sabotage-and-direct-hit
Can a player who isn't in the combat play Sabotage or Direct Hit?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2018-03-21

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-06-03

PorkStewJoe · Community · 2018-06-03

Sabotage, yes: any player can cancel another player's action card (other than a Sabotage). Direct Hit, no: the hit must have been produced by your own units or abilities, so a bystander has nothing to play it on.

### direct-hit-and-shields-holding-vs-ability-hits
Do Direct Hit and Shields Holding work on hits from Dimensional Splicer or Yin Devotion?

Designer ruling

The developer confirmed on BGG in 2018 that the earlier FAQ entry was a mistake; players relay his emailed rulings on both cards.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-05-24

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-23

Clipper · Community · 2018-05-23

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-05-24

pkitty · Community · 2017-12-16

shakesignal · Community · 2018-05-21disagrees

Yes to both. Direct Hit was errata'd to cover hits produced by your units or abilities, and Shields Holding can cancel these hits too. An early FAQ entry saying otherwise was acknowledged as a mistake by the developer, who confirmed the Splicer is meant to be that powerful.

### cripple-defenses-all-pds

### sabotaged-action-card-turn-not-used
If my component action card is cancelled by Sabotage, have I used my action for the turn?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-22

ronaldsf · Community · 2018-03-10

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-03-18

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-02

Quadrature_Strategy · Community · 2022-01-25

GAFBlizzard · Community · 2026-03-09

No — a cancelled card counts as not played, so you still take an action (or pass, if allowed). Anything that happened before you played it stands: if Flank Speed or Upgrade is Sabotaged after you activated a system, the system stays activated and you carry on without the bonus. If it was the optional extra action from Fleet Logistics, you can just end your turn.

### sabotage-timing
When exactly is Sabotage played: before or after the card's target is chosen?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

MATTPROV3 · Community · 2018-08-28

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-01-23

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-29

chrisdk · Community · 2023-02-12

hooliganj · Community · 2023-02-13

hooliganj · Community · 2018-08-28disagrees

After the player announces the card's choices (target planet, ship or player, rider prediction, retreat destination and so on), but before anything changes the game state, such as rolling dice or spending trade goods. Allow a short pause for it. Focused Research is the exception: its technology is chosen during resolution, after the Sabotage window.

### sabotage-what-it-cancels
Can Sabotage cancel another Sabotage or a promissory note?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-08

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-03-08

halbower · Community · 2017-12-02

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-02

No to both. Sabotage only cancels action cards, and never one named Sabotage. Other cancel effects that lack that restriction can still cancel a Sabotage.

### playing-action-cards-off-turn
Can I play action cards when it isn't my turn?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-11

AgentDrake · Community · 2019-02-11

Yes, whenever a card's timing text allows it — Sabotage can even go into a combat you aren't part of. Only cards whose timing is ACTION must be played as your action on your turn.

### upgrade-card-timing
When can the Upgrade action card be played?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2018-08-14

Weltenreiter · Community · 2018-08-14

Immediately after you activate a system containing your ships — not later in that tactical action, such as after producing a new cruiser.

### unstable-planet-up-to-three

Esau of Isaac · Community · 2018-01-10

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-03-18

Yes — it was errata'd to destroy up to 3 infantry, so it removes whatever is there, up to 3. It can also target an exhausted hazardous planet, since its effects are joined by 'and'.

### multiple-direct-hits-and-shields-holding
Can several Direct Hits or Shields Holdings be played in the same combat round?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

mjdomask · Community · 2017-12-20

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-14

zamoose · Community · 2023-03-23

hooliganj · Community · 2017-12-27

Clipper · Community · 2019-04-16

Odinatos · Community · 2017-12-14disagrees

Several Direct Hits, yes: each Sustain Damage use is its own trigger, so you can answer each with a Direct Hit on that ship. Sustain Damage is used unit by unit, so you can wait to see a Direct Hit before sustaining with more; hits already cancelled stay cancelled. Shields Holding: one per player against a set of incoming hits, though each combatant can play one.

No — like other action cards it resolves once, against one round's hits, and is discarded.

### parley-timing
When can Parley be played, and does it stop Bombardment?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-14

ksfga · Community · 2018-02-26

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-26

Crikrunner · Community · 2017-11-29

Dulkal · Community · 2017-11-29

Right after ground forces are committed to a planet, not later in the combat. Bombardment happens before that, so Parley can't stop it. The committed forces go back to space and that planet's invasion is off; to try again you need the system free of your command token.

### shields-holding-which-hits
Which hits can Shields Holding cancel?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-17

sigmazero13 · Community · 2020-02-26

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-03

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-15

Amuk · Community · 2018-03-18

Hits during space combat, including Anti-Fighter Barrage in the first round and ability hits like Dimensional Splicer, Ambush or Devotion. Not Space Cannon Offense or Defense, which happen outside space combat, and not 'destroy' effects such as Courageous to the End. Each combatant may play one in the same round.

### combat-cards-only-own-units
Can I play Direct Hit, Maneuvering Jets or other combat cards in a combat I'm not part of?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

donmakaron · Community · 2020-10-26

hooliganj · Community · 2020-10-26

MollyMae · Community · 2019-01-21

Clipper · Community · 2020-03-06

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-30

sigmazero13 · Community · 2017-11-26

In practice no — Direct Hit needs a hit produced by your own units or abilities, Maneuvering Jets only cancels hits on your own units, and the other combat cards refer to you, your units or your opponent. Bystanders can still play Sabotage, and a few cards like Scramble Frequency.

No — Direct Hit is played as Sustain Damage is used, while hits are being assigned; Emergency Repairs comes at the end of the round, too late.

Yes — all of that ability's dice are rolled together, so the card rerolls all of them, across different values or bombarded planets.

### promissory-notes-optional-to-play
Do I have to play a promissory note, such as Ceasefire, as soon as its timing comes up?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

csouth154 · Community · 2019-04-29

AgentDrake · Community · 2026-02-21

Clipper · Community · 2020-12-27

Falcone1983 · Community · 2017-11-29

hooliganj · Community · 2017-08-26

No — promissory notes are never mandatory unless the note says to play it immediately or place it face up. You can hold it and decide each time its timing window arises. This means the holder may choose not to play it; it does not let the note's owner refuse to honor it once played.

Yes — the reroll still applies. The trade-good loss and the reroll are printed as separate paragraphs, so they are separate abilities, and the reroll does not depend on the Letnev being able to pay.

No — each of these notes is a single paragraph where the benefit follows from removing the owner's strategy token, so if there is no token to remove, nothing on it resolves. The separate 'return this card' paragraph is judged on its own.

### sol-military-support-rules

### support-for-the-throne-return
When must I return another player's Support for the Throne?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-02

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-02

TomBoombs · Community · 2018-03-19

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-19

As soon as you activate a system containing any of that player's units, whether or not you move in or attack. Units include their ships, ground forces, space docks and PDS, but not control tokens, so activating a system where they only have control tokens is fine. Putting a command token in a system by other means is not activating it.

### unplayed-note-ignores-return-condition

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-09-01

No — a note you have not played face up ignores its own return conditions. But it also doesn't protect you from Pillage until it is face up in your play area.

### creuss-iff-placement
Can the Creuss IFF note put a wormhole token in a system with enemy ships if I control a planet there?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2019-07-14

zamoose · Community · 2019-09-12

csouth154 · Community · 2019-09-12

Yes — the two placement options are alternatives: a system containing a planet you control, or a non-home system with no other players' ships. Meeting either is enough, so enemy ships or a blockade don't matter if you control a planet there.

### ceasefire-timing

Geoff Watson · Community · 2019-07-01

Right after the system is activated, before any ships move; once ships have moved it's too late. The active player doesn't have to announce what will move first, and their own after-activation cards like Flank Speed get the first chance in that window.

### l1z1x-harrow-rules
How does the L1Z1X Harrow ability work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

pkitty · Community · 2017-12-16

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-16

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-02-19

Clipper · Community · 2018-02-20

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-05

Kesh · Community · 2018-02-14disagrees

Per errata it triggers at the end of each round of ground combat, as Bombardment from L1Z1X ships in the active system. Every ship with Bombardment there can join in, not just those that bombarded that planet. It still resolves after the round in which L1Z1X lose their last ground force; if it then kills the last defender, the combat is a draw. Planetary Shield stops it.

### jol-nar-flagship-hits

Each die showing 9 or 10 produces its normal hit plus 2 more, so its two dice can make up to 6 hits.

### hacan-flagship-trade-goods
How does the Hacan flagship's trade-good bonus work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-11

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-12-21

Clipper · Community · 2020-07-14

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-09-02

Roll all your combat dice first, then spend 1 trade good for +1 on any of your units' dice in its system, at most once per die. It only modifies combat rolls, not Space Cannon or other ability rolls.

### letnev-munitions-reserves
Can Letnev use Munitions Reserves every round, and does it cover Anti-Fighter Barrage?

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

chrisdk · Community · 2023-01-08

IncrediSteve · Community · 2023-01-08

hooliganj · Community · 2023-01-08

Every round, paying 2 trade goods each time, to reroll any of their dice that round. Whether it covers Anti-Fighter Barrage is debated; the better-supported view is yes, since barrage is part of the first combat round.

### jol-nar-fragile-fighters

Yes, on a 10: the -1 makes it 9, which still meets the fighter's combat value of 9. Fragile only affects combat rolls, not Space Cannon, Bombardment or Anti-Fighter Barrage.

### non-euclidean-shielding

Clipper · Community · 2019-12-29

csouth154 · Community · 2019-09-12

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-20

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-08-20

Every time one of your units uses Sustain Damage, it cancels 2 hits instead of 1, and that unit can still be assigned one of the remaining hits. Unused cancellation is lost; it doesn't carry over to a later hit such as Devotion's.

### arborec-letani-production
How does Arborec infantry Production work?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Jimb0v · Community · 2017-09-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-09-18

IncrediSteve · Community · 2021-01-03

RoughFudge · Community · 2021-09-09

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-07

Clipper · Community · 2017-12-11

Each Letani Warrior I has Production 1 (II has 2) and can build any unit you can pay for, ships included, with no space dock; they still pay resources. Their Production pools with your other units in the system, and they can produce in a system they just moved into, or from aboard ships. Arborec space docks can never produce infantry, so infantry output is capped by the Letani Warriors' own Production. Blockaded Letani can only make ground forces.

### arborec-flagship-produce-five
How does the Arborec flagship's produce-5 ability work?

Crikrunner · Community · 2017-09-24

woofdine · Community · 2018-02-02

Right after you activate the system it is already in, before movement, you may produce up to 5 units there, paying as normal; it isn't the Production step, so the regular production step still follows later. It can't move in first and then produce. Structures can't be produced this way.

### hacan-arbiters-trading

Any number of action cards can go either way in a transaction the Hacan are part of, still within one transaction per player per turn. The Hacan, or anyone trading with them, can transact regardless of neighbors, on either player's turn, though the active player must be involved.

### mentak-pillage-limits
How often can the Mentak use Pillage, and does it work in the agenda phase against everyone?

Designer ruling

The developer ruled on the agenda-phase part directly on BGG in 2019; players agree on the rest.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2019-01-17

JesperPersson · Community · 2018-01-30

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-30

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-05-01

Severedeye · Community · 2019-01-17disagrees

Pillage triggers separately each time a neighbor gains trade goods or resolves a transaction and ends with 3 or more, so it can hit each qualifying neighbor, in any phase. But the agenda phase does not make everyone neighbors: transactions with non-neighbors are allowed then, yet Pillage still only works on the Mentak's actual neighbors, as the developer ruled.

### naalu-foresight-limits
How often can the Naalu use Foresight, and what triggers it?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-05-06

ajmarshall · Community · 2022-05-06

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-11-20

LovingAngel · Community · 2017-11-21

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-21

hooliganj · Community · 2020-01-26disagrees

As often as they have strategy tokens and an eligible system to move to — there is no per-round limit. It needs another player's ships to actually move into and stop in the Naalu ships' system: not a ship merely passing through, and not an activation with nothing moved in. A Naalu command token already in that system doesn't block it, just as it wouldn't block a retreat.

### nekro-flagship-ground-forces

They take part in the space combat as if they were ships, so the opponent can assign hits to them. They are not ships for any other purpose, so they don't count against your fleet pool or your ships' capacity.

Yes — Technological Singularity works once per combat, and each space combat and each ground combat is a separate combat. Note that if you bombard the last defender before landing, there is no ground combat on that planet.

### naalu-matriarch-fighters-ground-combat

No. They join ground combat with all bonuses, but per the errata they return to space when combat ends, before a winner is decided. If only fighters remain, the combat is a draw, Dacxive Animators doesn't trigger, and the defender keeps the planet. You need actual ground forces to take control.

### nekro-singularity-what-counts
Which kills trigger Nekro's Technological Singularity?

Clipper · Community · 2020-08-26

Clipper · Community · 2020-10-11

DrewDecimal · Community · 2020-10-11

Only an opponent's units destroyed during a space or ground combat the Nekro are part of; it triggers as soon as the unit dies. Kills from Space Cannon, Bombardment, or structures destroyed when control is established don't count, since those happen outside combat. Anti-Fighter Barrage is part of combat and counts.

### nekro-flagship-combat-details
How do Nekro ground forces fighting via the flagship count for other effects?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

divzip · Community · 2021-10-08

csouth154 · Community · 2019-09-10

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-11-26

hooliganj · Community · 2023-11-29

mliszewski · Community · 2020-06-22

They take part as ships for that combat, so they count as non-fighter ships for things like the Winnu flagship's extra dice (per the LRR FAQ) and probably Assault Cannon. Effects naming a specific ship type, like fighters or destroyers, never apply to them. Carried infantry can join too, then land and fight on the ground the same action. If Assault Cannon hits Nekro when the flagship is their only non-fighter ship, the flagship must go.

### mentak-ambush-and-fourth-moon

Ambush is an extra roll at the start of combat; those ships still roll normally from the first round. The Fourth Moon stops other players' Sustain Damage in any round it takes part in, including the round it is destroyed, because Sustain Damage is used before hits remove it.

### naalu-foresight-timing-and-destinations

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-04-20

Right after the other player's ships move in, before Space Cannon Offense, so the Naalu leave before PDS fire, and Space Cannon can't follow them. They may go to any adjacent system without other players' ships, including the one the attacker just vacated or one with only enemy structures, but can't land ground forces there. Intercept doesn't stop it, as it isn't a combat retreat.

Their own fighter combat value, upgrades included; they act as ground forces but aren't infantry. All ground forces are committed in one step before any combat, so the same fighters can't fight on two planets in one invasion.

### mentak-ambush-details

At the start of space combat, before Anti-Fighter Barrage, roll one die for each of up to 2 cruisers or destroyers, mixed as you like. Hits are removed at once and destroyed ships never fire back. ### nekro-gaining-technology

TomBoombs · Community · 2018-05-28

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-04-20

No — the Nekro take the matching card from their own technology deck, and gaining ignores prerequisites. The Nekro choose which eligible technology to take, and it works immediately, even for the rest of that combat. It triggers when any opponent's unit is destroyed in combat, even one the Yin sacrifice with Devotion or one killed by Assault Cannon or Anti-Fighter Barrage.

### nekro-valefar-what-can-be-copied
What can the Nekro copy with Valefar Assimilator?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-11

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-01-11

Clipper · Community · 2019-12-16

Clipper · Community · 2018-04-26

Clipper · Community · 2022-07-11

Marcial · Community · 2020-11-23

Faction technology cards another player has actually researched, unit upgrades included, two at a time, one per token; to copy a third you move a token. Units printed on faction sheets, like Muaat's Prototype War Sun I or flagships, aren't technologies and can't be copied (the Nomad's Memoria II card is the exception). Copying a faction war sun technology does make the Nekro subject to Publicize Weapon Schematics.

### nekro-valefar-unit-upgrades

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-06

Nyarlathotep90 · Community · 2018-03-07disagrees

The copied faction upgrade covers your generic one, which you keep; only one is in effect. Move the token away and the generic upgrade applies again. A second faction upgrade for the same unit replaces the first, using the same token.

### nekro-losing-copied-tech
What happens when the Nekro move a Valefar token off a technology their units depend on?

The copied tech is swapped, not stacked. Lose a war sun technology with no replacement and all Nekro war suns are destroyed. Lose a copied Floating Factory II and each dock in space moves to a planet you control in its system, or is destroyed if none qualifies. Ships already in a supernova after losing Magmus Reactor are fine.

### mentak-pillage-triggers

Community consensus

IncrediSteve · Community · 2022-12-29

pkitty · Community · 2022-08-23

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-04

After each instance of a neighbor gaining trade goods or resolving a transaction, if that neighbor then has 3 or more trade goods and no face-up Promise of Protection. Both sides of a transaction between two neighbors can each be pillaged, in the order Mentak chooses, and receiving converted commodities counts as gaining trade goods. Nobody can dump trade goods between the trigger and Pillage. A commodity taken becomes a trade good.

### yin-indoctrination-wins-combat

Yes — Indoctrination is used once the ground combat has begun, so if it removes the last defender the Yin win, which counts for Dacxive Animators and 'win a combat' objectives, and any card the opponent played is wasted. If Bombardment had already killed every defender, there is no ground combat to win.

### saar-floating-factory-not-a-ship
Are Saar Floating Factories ships, and do they count against the fleet pool?

Designer ruling

The game's developer answered this directly on BGG in 2018.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-01-05

Pete Walsh · Community · 2018-01-01

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-01superseded

No — Floating Factories are never ships; they only move and retreat as if they were. They don't count against your fleet pool, but every ability that affects movement, such as Gravity Drive, Flank Speed, Antimass Deflectors or Light/Wave Deflector, still applies to them and the ships moving with them.

“The general movement of the Floating Factory and its accompanying ships (if any) shouldn’t be hindered by the fact that it is not technically a ship.”

— CreussEmissary, designer

### saar-floating-factory-space-cannon
Can Space Cannon fire at, or hits be assigned to, a Saar Floating Factory when it moves?

Designer ruling

The developer reversed his own earlier ruling directly on BGG in 2018 and moved it into the FAQ.

CreussEmissary · Designer · 2018-01-05

Padish · Community · 2017-12-20

halbower · Community · 2017-12-20

kaiteinomen · Community · 2018-03-12

Clipper · Community · 2018-01-01superseded

No — PDS cannot fire at a moving Floating Factory, and hits cannot be assigned to it because it is not a ship. The developer originally ruled the other way, then publicly reversed himself in January 2018 and put the corrected ruling in the FAQ.

“no—PDS cannot fire at a Floating Factory when it moves. I’ll make it clear in the FAQ.”

— CreussEmissary, designer

### saar-floating-factory-placement
Where do Saar Floating Factories go, and how many can share a system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

CplPunishment · Community · 2019-09-17

jradziew · Community · 2019-09-17

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-04-06

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-11

In the space area, never on a planet. If an effect places a space dock on a planet, choose the planet as the effect says, then the Floating Factory's own rule puts it in that system's space area. There is no limit on how many share a system, and their production value is fixed rather than based on a planet. A Floating Factory is destroyed if it is left without your ships against enemy ships.

### yssaril-stall-tactics

It lets Yssaril spend an action discarding an action card instead of passing, so they can wait for others to act first. It is a faction ability, not an action card, so Sabotage cannot cancel it.

### xxcha-nullification-field-timing
When must Xxcha use Nullification Field, and what can still happen after it ends the turn?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2023-07-23

Falkman · Community · 2023-07-24

Sabutai · Community · 2018-12-04

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-12-21

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-23

ajmarshall · Community · 2022-02-25

It must be used right after the system is activated, before movement; Xxcha may ask the active player to pause. The active player gets first turn in that window, so their own after-activation cards like In the Silence of Space or Flank Speed resolve first and stay spent. Once the turn ends, nothing more happens in it: no further after-activation abilities, no transactions and no extra action from Master Plan.

### yssaril-scheming-and-discards

No to both. Scheming only triggers when you draw action cards from the deck, not when you take or receive them from a player. Ignoring the hand limit doesn't stop effects that make you discard.

### yin-devotion-rules

IncrediSteve · Community · 2019-07-31

After a round of space combat, Yin may destroy one of their cruisers or destroyers in the active system — the errata adds 'in the active system' — to produce a hit that the Yin player assigns to an enemy ship. It is a single faction ability, so once per combat round. It has different timing from Impulse Core, so both can be used in one combat. Indoctrination works once per ground combat, at its start.

### saar-floating-factory-production
Can Saar Floating Factories move and produce in the same action, and how many can share a system?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2026-08-30

selwyth · Community · 2026-03-22

hooliganj · Community · 2017-11-22

IndyBart · Community · 2019-01-20

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-11-14

Yes — nothing requires a dock to start the turn in the active system, so a Floating Factory can move in, carry ground forces to invade, and produce. Any number can share a system, since they sit in space and aren't bound by one dock per planet, and two of them combine Production. A Floating Factory is blockaded, and destroyed, the moment enemy ships arrive without Saar ships.

### saar-chaos-mapping

Clipper · Community · 2019-09-10

Clipper · Community · 2018-08-19

At the start of each of your turns, including the turn you pass, you may produce one unit in a system with your Production units; one unit total, not one per system. It needs no command token and you still pay for it, but no unit's Production ability is used, so Sarween Tools doesn't apply.

### yin-spinner-versions

hooliganj · Community · 2021-08-02

The original gives one infantry per use of Production, however many units produced, on any planet you control in that system, and works with the Warfare secondary; it doesn't trigger from Integrated Economy, which uses no Production ability. The Omega version gives 2 infantry, triggers whenever you produce, and changes placement.

### game-end-without-10-points
When does the game end if nobody reaches 10 victory points?

Per the rulebook — Winning the Game

The rulebook covers this under "Winning the Game".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Winning the Game

IncrediSteve · Community · 2017-08-16

Clipper · Community · 2020-10-05

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-27

CBpegasus · Community · 2017-12-15

When the speaker has to reveal a public objective in the status phase and none remain. The game ends right there, with no agenda phase, and the player with the most points wins. With five stage I and five stage II objectives, that is round 9 at the latest.

### two-players-reach-10-in-status-phase
If two players could both reach 10 points in the same status phase, who wins?

Per the rulebook — Object of the Game

The rulebook covers this under "Object of the Game".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Object of the Game

TomBoombs · Community · 2018-05-22

mliszewski · Community · 2018-07-30

Clipper · Community · 2018-07-30

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-11-09

Ragnarwarbar · Community · 2018-07-30disagrees

The first one in initiative order. Objectives are scored one player at a time in initiative order and the game ends the instant someone reaches the goal, so later players never score. The speaker-order tiebreak only covers truly simultaneous gains.

### simultaneous-10-points-agenda-phase
If two players reach 10 points at the same moment in the agenda phase, who wins?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

alercah · Community · 2017-11-22

Technopinky · Community · 2017-11-19

hooliganj · Community · 2018-08-01

Clipper · Community · 2020-04-29

IncrediSteve · Community · 2018-01-07

Dalek5 · Community · 2017-11-19disagrees

Start with the speaker and go clockwise: the tied speaker wins, otherwise the first tied player clockwise from the speaker. Strategy cards have no bearing in the agenda phase, so initiative order doesn't apply.

### support-for-the-throne-swap-win
If two players swap Support for the Throne and both would hit 10, who wins?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

hooliganj · Community · 2019-07-14

Geoff Watson · Community · 2019-07-13

Clipper · Community · 2019-07-15

hooliganj · Community · 2019-07-18

Only one player can win. The swapped notes resolve one after the other in normal timing order, so the player who gains the point first wins and the game ends before the other scores. There are no shared victories.

### winning-during-action-phase
Can a player win during the action phase?

Per the rulebook — Winning the Game

The rulebook covers this under "Winning the Game".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Winning the Game

sigmazero13 · Community · 2019-10-07

ysirian · Community · 2019-10-07

Yes — the game ends the moment anyone reaches the goal. Points can come in the action phase from action-phase secret objectives, the Imperial card (including scoring a public objective), the custodians token and some agendas.

### elimination-conditions
When is a player eliminated?

Per the rulebook — Elimination

The rulebook covers this under "Elimination".

Twilight Imperium: Fourth Edition rulebook · Rulebook · Elimination

Clipper · Community · 2019-09-10

Only when all three are true: no ground forces on the board, no units with Production, and no planets controlled. Ships alone don't keep you in the game if the rest is gone.

### strategy-cards-after-elimination
After a player is eliminated, do the remaining players pick more strategy cards?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

IncrediSteve · Community · 2025-03-03

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-10-15

Clipper · Community · 2018-10-15

Clipper · Community · 2019-02-05

Crikrunner · Community · 2018-08-06

SnakePlissken72 · Community · 2025-03-03disagrees

No — keep the strategy card rules for the player count you started with, so a five-player game doesn't become a four-player game; the extra cards just collect trade goods. An eliminated player's unused strategy card is never resolved and returns at the end of the round, and agendas they owned are discarded.

### eliminated-player-tokens
When a player is eliminated, do their wormhole tokens or Naalu 0 token leave the game?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

sigmazero13 · Community · 2018-03-12

Clipper · Community · 2018-03-12

MollyMae · Community · 2018-03-12

Not automatically — the elimination rule doesn't list them, so placed Creuss wormhole tokens stay on the board, and a Naalu 0 token held by another player through the promissory note stays until the end of that round.

### eliminated-player-assimilated-tech
What happens to a technology the Nekro copied with Valefar Assimilator if its owner is eliminated?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Vidyaraja · Community · 2017-09-18

ThierryV · Community · 2017-09-19

The Nekro takes that technology card into their play area and keeps it until they move the token or the game ends; then it returns to the box with the eliminated player's components.

### checks-and-balances-selection
How does strategy card selection work under Checks and Balances?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

DistantOasis · Community · 2021-09-12

Clipper · Community · 2021-09-09

Turn order stays the same, but on your pick you give the card you choose to a player who doesn't yet have their full number of cards. Only if nobody else can take it, as for the last picker, do you keep it.

### political-censure-revote
If Miscount Disclosed revotes Political Censure, who gains or loses points?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-19

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-20

TomBoombs · Community · 2021-08-20

Political Censure says you lose its point when you lose the card, so the previous owner loses 1 and the new owner gains 1; the same player re-elected loses and regains it. That differs from Shard of the Throne and similar laws.

### keleres-mech-influence-per-mech

Yes — each mech is its own instance of the ability, so the attacker pays 1 influence per mech. The influence can come from any mix of planets and trade goods; it doesn't have to be one planet per mech.

### keleres-hero-choice

Through The Tribunii: at setup they pick an unused faction from Mentak, Xxcha and Argent Flight and take that faction's home system, tokens and matching Keleres hero.

### naalu-omega-agent-tactical-action

No token is spent from the tactic pool. The tactical action happens during the Naalu player's turn, so an effect that ends the turn, like Xxcha's Nullification Field, ends the Naalu turn and the borrowed tactical action with it.

### mahact-decoy-operation-starlancer

Probably yes. Both trigger after the system is activated, and a player resolving several abilities in one window chooses their order, so Decoy Operation can go first and the Starlancer ability second. Some players think it fails, by analogy with other rulings.

### xxcha-agent-other-players-planet

### capturing-fighters-and-infantry
How do you capture fighters and infantry?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-28

Clipper · Community · 2021-08-28

AgentDrake · Community · 2021-08-21

Only the Vuil'raith capture units, through their own abilities (or a faction copying those). A captured ship or mech goes on the captor's faction sheet. A captured fighter or infantry goes back to its owner's reinforcements as if destroyed, and the captor places a matching token on their faction sheet instead; those tokens still count for Cabal abilities.

### titans-terragenesis-limits

No to both. Terragenesis triggers after you explore a planet without a sleeper and places one token, so a planet holds at most one. Mecatol Rex and home planets have no trait and can't be explored, so they never get one this way.

### cabal-fleet-destroyed-by-yin-flagship
Does the Vuil'raith flagship capture units destroyed at the same moment it is destroyed?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree and relay an emailed ruling from FFG; no official post on BGG.

Ippiphel · Community · 2022-11-11

hooliganj · Community · 2024-10-27

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-05superseded

MollyMae · Community · 2024-10-27disagrees

Yes, according to the developer's later FAQ answers that players compiled: it captures every other unit destroyed in the same timing window, even if it is destroyed there too, such as by the Yin flagship or Exotrireme II; it never captures itself. That reversed an earlier answer, and some players still disagree. If the flagship was destroyed earlier, it isn't there to capture later losses.

### argent-raid-formation-no-fighters
How does Argent Flight's Raid Formation work, including against an opponent with no fighters?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

AgentDrake · Community · 2025-03-24

IndyBart · Community · 2021-01-05

ajmarshall · Community · 2021-04-15

Sir Gerard · Community · 2026-01-10

hooliganj · Community · 2026-01-10

Anti-Fighter Barrage hits beyond the number of enemy fighters damage that many enemy ships with Sustain Damage, chosen by the Argent player. It produces no hits and those ships don't use Sustain Damage, so it can't destroy anything and Direct Hit or Non-Euclidean Shielding don't come into it. Argent fire barrage even when the opponent has no fighters, making every hit excess.

### legendary-ability-card-ready
Is a legendary planet's ability card exhausted when I take the planet?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

ajmarshall · Community · 2020-11-29

sigmazero13 · Community · 2022-06-05

LovingAngel · Community · 2020-12-22

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-22

The planet card arrives exhausted as usual, but the legendary ability card arrives ready, unless you take it from another player, in which case it keeps its state. It works independently of the planet: you can use it after spending the planet, effects that ready the planet don't ready it, and it isn't an attachment.

### titans-awaken-timing
When can the Titans use Awaken, and can Ceasefire or Nullification Field stop it?

Community consensus

Several experienced players agree; there is no official ruling on BGG.

TreacleMiner · Community · 2021-04-15

Wakke · Community · 2021-06-16

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-07

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-12-08

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-22

Jasteni · Community · 2021-06-14disagrees

Right after activating a system that already held your sleeper token; a sleeper placed later in that activation, say by Scanlink exploration, can't be awakened then. Ceasefire only stops movement, so Awaken still works. Awaken and Nullification Field share the after-activation window, and the active Titans go first, so they can awaken before their turn is ended; the resulting ground combat still happens.

### titans-scanlink-awaken-terragenesis
Can Titans explore a planet with Scanlink, Awaken its sleeper, and then use Terragenesis there?

Best reading — unconfirmed

Players disagree; this is the better-supported and more recent reading, with no official ruling.

zamoose · Community · 2026-08-27

zamoose · Community · 2026-08-27

hooliganj · Community · 2022-07-19

hooliganj · Community · 2022-07-19

IncrediSteve · Community · 2020-12-12disagrees

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-12disagrees

Most likely not. Scanlink's 'when you activate' exploration comes before the 'after' window, so when you explore, the sleeper is still on the planet and Terragenesis can't trigger; later answers also say you can't place a sleeper with Terragenesis and Awaken it in the same action. Some 2020 readings allowed Scanlink, then Terragenesis, then Awaken. You can, however, Scanlink first and then Awaken before Xxcha's Nullification Field.

### vuilraith-dimensional-tear-rifts
Do Vuil'raith ships roll for Cormund's gravity rift if their Dimensional Tear dock is there, and do two tears stack?

AgentDrake · Community · 2020-12-05

hooliganj · Community · 2020-12-05

No roll: a system with more than one rift counts as one gravity rift, and Dimensional Tear says your ships don't roll for that system's rift. Two tears in one system don't stack the bonus either; it is simply a gravity rift, and counts as an anomaly for objectives.

### supernova-planets-and-activation
